into my arms

by bitchywitchyboo

Summary

Agatha and Rio's one-night stand has disastrous consequences.

OR

That enchanted strap worked a little too well and our favourite witches are forced to confront their fourteen-year history with one another, and learn to let the past go as they build a new future together (much to Agatha's dismay and all for Rio's pleasure).

Modern Day, Witchy, and an undisclosed other that you will have to work out for yourselves otherwise it ruins the entire fic AU.

Chapter 1

Chapter Summary

Agatha and Rio bump into each other at a bar, for the first time in two years.

Chapter Notes

u see smut and i gently remind you its a slow(ish) burn

If Agatha had known that entering the bar that evening would lead her to losing everything that kept her going in this sorry excuse for a life, she would have swiftly turned on her heel and ran into oncoming traffic. 

Unfortunately, hindsight had not been on her side that night. 

She had reluctantly agreed to join her colleagues for a drink after work for two reasons: in her appraisal last week her boss had described her work as exemplary but her ability to be a “team player” as questionable, and secondly, it had been a day. A long, nauseating day. 

Plus, her colleagues were old, bald and desperate to avoid their wives so she hardly had to raise a finger in order to get a free drink. 

She had turned up late, as planned. Norm from HR had gotten her one drink whilst her editor, Phil, had begrudgingly provided the second. 

She was not made for small talk, which is why she had chosen to become a journalist and not a daytime television chat-show host. Clearly she had missed the contractual fine-print that specified she must be a corporate leech who knew when to smile and when to nod whilst also uncovering some of the sketchiest cases of fraud that the twenty-first century had ever seen. 

It would have been easier to just conjure a memory of her presence that night in each of their minds, but she would have ended up in the same haunt in search of something to keep her occupied for the evening regardless. 

Two drinks, she had told herself. Two drinks, maximum, before their pores would drip with sweat and their ties were long discarded, perving on some blonde young enough to be their granddaughter. Then it was her time to play. 

Of course, first, she had to find a toy to play with. That was always the hardest part, not that it was hard for Agatha to score. 

She took pride in captivating women like putty in her hands, but there had been no worse a day than when she walked into the office to find the woman she had been four digits deep in just one week previously, sat next to a man she claimed to be her husband no less, with legal representation beside them as they threatened to sue their paper for defamation of said husband and his tax-evading investment company. 

Safe to say, the odds were against them on that case from the get-go. 

From that day on, she had rules: no corporate leeches (aside from herself), no wedding rings (there had not been one for her to see) and absolutely no children in the picture (just because). 

None of those things found her that night. Unfortunately, what found her was worse. 

“Fucking hell, you can’t be serious.” 

The hair on her neck heard the voice before her ears did. That voice was both unnervingly familiar and annoyingly enticing. 

“We really must stop running into each other like this, Agatha.” 

As she expected, upon looking up to the presence felt over her shoulder, she was met with brown eyes and a smirk Agatha fancied wiping off her face. 

“Rio?” Agatha breathed. 

She had not seen Rio for two years. It was impressive they had managed that long, seeing as they lived in the same city. Not that the city was small, but still, Agatha was thankful she had managed that long at least. 

Rio’s eyes had a glint to them that Agatha remembered as being distinctly her. Honestly, it was a sight she never could forget. They had a life to them that only emphasised the death of what had been.

Without invitation, Rio slid into the booth beside her. She kept her distance, Agatha noted, before realising her own breath had stopped until the woman had taken her seat. 

She looked good, too. Really good. She had ditched the dungarees and beanie for an oversized blazer and side-bangs. What Agatha remembered as being hyperactive had been replaced with an aura she couldn’t quite put her finger on. 

“Ah, she remembers.” 

“No, I just thought of the most popular name I know, which of course, would be the really well-known, overused name that is ‘Rio’,” Agatha retorted. “Yeah, there are at least five of them on my office floor alone.” 

Rio rolled her eyes. “Still got a stick up your ass, I see.” 

“We haven’t seen each other for years and you’re already talking about my ass?”

The woman opposite her huffed a laugh, her tongue poking against the inside of her cheek as her head shook in disbelief. 

“I’ll stop talking about it if you let me buy you a drink.” 

Big mistake, number one. 

Rio had offered to cross that inevitable line that they always teetered too close to when together. 

Agatha took a slow sip of wine. She studied the woman before her, hesitant to offer an immediate reply. 

Her voice lowered. “That’s not going to end well.”

“Is it not?”

“You know as well as I do.” 

Rio didn’t believe her. Agatha didn’t really believe herself. 

“Is that right, Harkness?” Rio’s eyes darkened, moving to take Agatha’s left hand into her own, inspecting her fingers for proof that she should just accept that the past was in the past. “Stilll a Harkness, hm? No wife waiting for you at home? Don’t fancy a fight tonight - this jacket is new.” 

“Darling, did I ever give you the impression that I would want a wife?” 

A brief pause settled between them, the clinking of glasses echoed behind them as the jukebox increased its tempo. 

“Girlfriend?” 

“No one is coming for your damn jacket, Rio.” 

Rio simply smirked. “So, how about that drink?” 

Agatha considered her answer. 

A drink with Rio, discuss those times without actually discussing them, get drunk enough to wake up the next morning and claim its best to leave the past in the past, and move on with their lives whilst both being left somewhat satisfied. 

No drink with Rio, and she was left in exactly the same position she had started that evening in. The thought was worse than spending the night with an ex-flame who she already knew was more than capable of making her forget about everything else, at least. 

Absolutely not. 

Besides, she’d be lying if she hadn’t thought once or twice - or several times more - about Rio in their absence of friendship, if you could call it that. 

You most definitely could not call it that. 

At least she’d have something fun to tell Jen. 

On second thoughts, Jen was never finding out about this. For both of their sakes.

Rio looked at her with an expression that suggested she would be happy eating shit. 

Perhaps she wasn’t the only one that had reminisced on old times shared together. Even if the memories were somewhat hazy for both parties.

From memory, the space had been cluttered in a way that felt inviting and not constricting. It had to be that, and absolutely nothing to do with the fact that Agatha just could not find a better person to get laid by other than Rio Vidal.

Control was good. Control avoided hurt. 

Or so she hoped.

But hope was a liar.

Some nights, Agatha would soon learn, did not end just because she decided they would. 

 

Chapter 2

Chapter Summary

Jen drags Agatha to a bar. Agatha ends up leaving to get high with Rio. Oh, and you're going to want to read until the end.

Chapter Notes

lets get the party [story] going

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Fuck. 

Jen. 

She was supposed to be at brunch, with Jen, sixteen minutes ago.

“Agatha!” 

Yes, there it was.

Against her wishes, the events of the night before played out like some stupid montage that made her consider going back to therapy. She could feel Rio’s hands on her hips, the way her nails sunk into her skin. Those brown, pleading eyes kept looking up at her and she cursed herself for imagining the way they would have looked that morning - the way they would have looked at her - had she not left. 

Thank god she had left.

A sigh escaped her throat, before untangling herself from the duvet and inhaling sharply at the cold she was met with. The room moved with her, forcing her hand against the wall to gain composure and keep the nausea threatening to rise in her throat, at bay. 

It was Rio’s fault. 

Rio and her stupid off-brand tequila that may as well have been ninety-percent white spirit.

One nice thing Jen had done was choose the closest coffee shop to Agatha’s apartment building. Only one block away, the brief walk was short enough to help how she was feeling rather than hinder it.  

Still, it was a Saturday morning and her patience was quickly wearing thin. She had already witnessed three fully grown adults do a photoshoot with their orders, two children were running from table to table without a guardian in sight, and one baby’s screams echoed exactly how Agatha’s head still felt. 

“So, what are you doing three Friday’s from now?” 

“None of your business, and I’d like to keep it that way.” 

Jen’s smile was falsely sweet. “I’ve been invited to an event. A launch of some new brand that found my page. You’re coming with me.” 

“No.”

“I’ll call ahead. The day before, morning of, and after you leave the office. Not having a repeat of today.” 

“Oh my god. I had a few drinks - sue me! I thought you were the one always banging on at me about getting out there-”

“Oh, Agatha. We both know you don’t have a problem with getting ‘out there’ as you so eloquently put it. It's… you should be looking for company on a… longer-term basis.”

“Hah,” Agatha spat. “Rich coming from you.” 

Jen shot her a glare. “I am very capable of indulging in long-term company, thank you.”

Agatha chose not to enquire further. Her cappuccino was doing little for the headache, and the lights were insistent on reminding her just how much cheap tequila she had consumed the night before. “This event. It’s not going to be some orgy of women in pink pyjamas putting face-masks on and posing in front of a wall that looks like someone threw up flowers on it, right?” 

Jen offered a mock gasp. “What do you take me for?” 

Agatha made a point to look at the woman before her, up and down; every piece of clothing and accessory she adorned some variation of blush. “A midwife who has a skincare business on the side and chooses to look like a human marshmallow everyday?” 

Jen simply stared at her. 

“Oh, sorry,” Agatha continued with an expression that could have given a sad child a run for their money.  “Was that rhetorical?” 

“How many times do I have to tell you - I’m an OBGYN.” 

“Yes, we get it. You love vaginas. Same thing.”

“No, actually-” 

Agatha held her hand up in Jen’s face. Her face hardened, eyes daring Jen to continue. 

“It's a clothing brand. Womenswear. Elle has featured them before. They’ve hired out a bar-”

“An open bar?”

Agatha should have seen the sigh coming. 

“It starts at nine. Dress nicely.” 

“I always dress nicely."

“This morning’s look begs to differ,” Jen replied too quickly. She sipped at her matcha, before looking at Agatha with a look that immediately set her on edge. She knew that look: the softening of Jen’s eyes, the hesitation to say what she clearly was going to say anyway. “Oh, I know you won’t want to talk about it but have you got plans for Christmas yet? I know it's a couple of months away, but… I don’t want you on your own again.” 

Agatha could have sworn her stomach was in her throat. Jen had excellent timing, she had to give her that. Nothing like bringing up the one thing she knew Agatha would not want to discuss, especially when she already felt like the human embodiment of disappointment. 

Besides, she did not even see the need for such a holiday, especially as an adult and especially when you only had adults to share it with.  

“I’ll be with Mom, but you know she’d love to have you-”

“I’ll be with Lilia,” Agatha may as well have thrown the words at Jen, they were that abrupt. 

The room seemed to quieten despite the noise continuing. The whir of the coffee grinder faded, whilst the baby’s screams only seemed to get closer and Agatha became quickly aware of the heat rising against her neck. 

She did not want to be with Lilia. She did not want to be with anyone. It seemed the one person who was determined to be on her own, actually had a small village of people ensuring she never would be. It was an odd realisation, considering she was a shit excuse for a witch, and an even shittier excuse for a person. 

A hand found Agatha’s knee. Agatha’s eyes snapped up at Jen, her entire body freezing at the touch. “You don’t have to do this alone,” Jen’s smile had turned sincere. 

Agatha remained still. She was all too aware of the hand that touched her and the quickening beat in her chest. She hated when Jen did this; hated that she could not enjoy one moment with her friend without it becoming an intervention. 

“Are you done?” 

Agatha’s hardened expression and pointed tone left little room for argument, even if her own stomach dropped slightly at the sight of Jen recoiling. 

Jen quickly took her hand away, offering a polite nod. That was the problem with Jen and the problem with Lilia. The problem with anyone who knew her. No matter how much she pushed them away, they saw straight through her. 

“Three weeks time. I’ll send you the details soon. Please don’t kill yourself between now and then. Or get an STD. Oh, actually, if you ever need to get checked-” 

“You’ll be the last person I come too.” 

Jen sipped the last of her drink before placing the mug onto the table and gathering her scarf and coat. “There she is,” she smiled before bending down and placing a quick kiss to Agatha’s cheek. Naturally, Agatha gagged in response. “I’m busy next weekend, but I’ll call you to check in.” 

 

 

The weeks passed slowly. It was that time of year where the world was on its last legs. The festive period approached and with it brought a permanent fatigue that Agatha could not shake. 

She hated this time of year; hated realising that it was one more trip around the sun, taking her further and further away from everything she could have had. 

Work provided a constant distraction, despite having to navigate the impending festive season politics that she frankly did not give a damn about.

One of her pieces caused a stir in The New Yorker, and was picked up by CNN. The attention on herself was unwanted, but she was pleased to see the prick that said-piece was about being dragged through the media for cutting funds at the children’s hospital oncology department. For his own vacation, no less. 

As promised, Jen had already called her three times that day. Not to mention the amount of texts she had received from her everyday that week. 

She stood before the tall standing mirror in the living room, fixing her earrings and taking in her final look. She looked good, even she would admit. Her co-ord was pinstriped, blazer undone to reveal a white tank. She had slicked her hair into a ponytail and left her makeup simple: blush, mascara, something on her lips. Enough to convince an untrained eye that she had not woken up feeling like getting out of bed should be an option. 

As predicted, she was chaperoned door-to-door by Jen. The event itself was held downtown and upon arriving, Agatha was already calculating how quickly she could leave unnoticed. 

The bar was typical of any establishment trying to lure in the kind of person who would broadcast it all over their social media: an entire wall of oddly shaped mirrors, a photobooth, and one neon ‘girls just wanna have fun’ sign that made her skin crawl. 

It was the type of crowd at the event that left her most on edge. At thirty-six, Agatha did not claim to be all-knowing, but here? Here she felt ancient. The other guests couldn’t have been much younger, nor older, than her but they were certainly cut from a different cloth. A more liberal cloth - one that made her realise just how much her own mother’s values had rubbed off on her, despite her best efforts not to succumb to them. They each had a happiness in their self-expression that Agatha could only admire from afar, despite the deep-seated unease that took over her. 

It was when Agatha noticed the mannequins and clothing rails showcasing the brand’s new line, that she realised there may have been an ulterior motive for her best friend to want to attend this event. In fact, Agatha highly doubted they had even scouted Jen out. Rather than tasteful dresses and tailored pantsuits, she was met with miniskirts that left her questioning how they would actually hide what they were supposed to hide. There were fishnet tights, neon leg warmers and an alarming amount of rhinestone and leather. 

“This brand really found your page and decided ‘oh yeah, her audience is definitely our demographic’?” Agatha remarked as her hands brushed against the leather of a bike jacket that read Bad Girl on the back. 

“You’d be surprised,” Jen moved towards the bar, inspecting the drinks menu. “I have a surprisingly far reach. Drink?”

“Something strong, something-” Agatha looked at the drinks menu before sighing. “What the fuck is a ‘monster margarita’? Jen, where the hell have you brought me?” 

She should have noticed the way Jen’s eyes refused to meet her own. The way Jen kept glancing around the room, fiddling with her earrings or straightening her dress out one too many times, but the music was beating in her ears and Agatha was too aware of the hoards of strangers in tutus that surrounded her.

“I’m pretty sure the forty-somthing-plus peri-menopausal women who use your slug-sperm-whatever it is on their faces would be put to an early grave if they saw you attending this.” 

“Being a bit judgemental, aren’t we Agatha?” 

Agatha snarled under her breath. She haggled with the bartender to make her something normal, and stiff. The first sip of her bourbon was enough for her shoulders to ease slightly. “I’m going outside. Stay here.” 

“I thought you packed that in!” 

“So did I, but I was unaware you’d bring me to a pre-pubescent sex show.” 

Her cigarette did not do much to make her feel better. In fact, she only felt worse. 

It was when she walked back into the bar that she realised why Jen had been so insistent on them attending. Someone was speaking over the microphone, and she only caught the last few words of the announcement, but it was enough to make her freeze entirely. 

“- to present onto the decks, Alice Wu-Gulliver.”

“You have got to be fucking kidding me,” she muttered under her breath as she watched the short woman still complete with spikey, coloured hair and piercings head-to-toe set her headphones upon her head and offer a wave to the growing crowd before mixing in her first track of the night. 

Agatha bee-lined straight for Jen. 

“So was your invite from the brand, or the really hot DJ you used to fuck ten years ago? Oh, and when the fuck were you planning on telling me?”

“Purely coincidental.” 

“Don’t bullshit me.” 

“You would never have agreed to come.” 

“Too right I wouldn’t have. So I’m just here to… what? Be your wing-woman? Make her get jealous? Come on, I know I would stoop this low. But you?” 

Jen did not reply. 

Agatha laughed as she studied the situation further. “Are we even invited? Or are we risking being kicked out from an event that I didn’t even want to show up to in the first place. Jesus Jen, low blow.” 

Jen’s lips were pursed. “We are invited. Alice invited me.”

“You hate her!” 

“Hated.” 

Silence fell between the two best friends. Agatha finished her bourbon and immediately ordered a second. She watched the way Jen looked at Alice from across the room. Her skin became slightly too hot and the urge to throw something became overwhelming. 

“How long?” Agatha asked through pursed lips. 

“A couple of months.” 

“And she’s just magically forgotten about you cheating-”

“I did not cheat.”

“Alice would say differently.” 

“Well it's lucky for her that the only adultery I committed was with my medical degree. It's not my fault she got her pants in a-”

“Am I interrupting?” a voice from behind them asked as it cut off Jen. Agatha should have thanked the universe for its intervention, but how could she be thankful at hearing that voice? “I thought I said we must stop running into each other like this.” 

Agatha did not even bother to turn around. “Oh for fucks sake.” 

“Hi Jen,” Rio was far too up-beat for the situation they found themselves in. “Good to see you! Mind if I steal this one for a bit?” 

Agatha was the first to speak. “Yes, yes I do mind. Very much actually.” 

Rio turned to Jen. The woman in pink had been watching their exchange with intrigue, and Agatha feared she would cotton on to the fact that she had not been the only one between them reigniting flames that should absolutely remain dead in the water.  

“Be my guest.” 

Agatha wished she could slap the smirk from Jen’s face. 

She turned to Rio - to that goofy grin and bright eyes looking up at her like she expected the goddamn world. A wave of nausea rolled through her stomach; she hoped it would stop cowering away and give her a decent enough excuse to leave the evening entirely. “Get me another drink first.” 

“It took a lot more convincing than that last time.” 

Jen looked between them both, eyebrows raised. “Last time?” 

Agatha kicked her heel into Rio’s shin, before smiling with a sickening sweetness at her best friend. “She’s clearly still hung up on the past. Looks like you two have that in common.” 

“It was great seeing you, Jen. Let me take this one off your hands. Let her cool off a bit.” 

Jen laughed under her breath. “Good luck.” 

Agatha did not expect Rio to put her arm around her shoulder, and begin physically removing her from the situation they found themselves in. She turned over her shoulder to see Jen looking at her, eyebrow still quirked and a menacing grin that may as well have screamed at her: “‘we are so talking about this later”. Agatha simply shot her a glare back.

“Where are you taking me?” 

Rio was leading her through the crowds of people, past the pounding stereos and towards the smoking area. “To cool off.”

“I said I wanted a drink.” 

“Don’t start that again.” 

The cold air was a welcome reprieve from the sweatbox the bar was already turning into. “I’m being serious, Rio. It's the least you can do before you kidnap me.”

“Oh, so I am allowed to kidnap you?” 

God damn this woman, Agatha thought. 

“If there is a bottle of bourbon on offer.” 

“Come with me,” Rio took her hand and led her to the front entrance where more punters still awaited their entry.  

“This doesn’t look like a place where I can order a drink.” 

Rio walked away from Agatha without another word. She moved to the bouncers on the door, and greeted them both like old friends, sharing a brief conversation before moving back to her kidnapee. She took a pack of cigarettes from the inside of her balcony overcoat and offered them to Agatha. “You still smoke, right?” 

Agatha took a cigarette and placed it between her lips without responding. She looked at the woman expectantly, deciding not to reach for her own lighter. Thankfully, chivalry was not dead; Rio took out her own and edged closer before lighting it for her. 

Several moments passed between ash and smoke. One of the bouncers returned with a rather expensive looking japanese malt in hand. He passed it to Rio, both exchanging brief pleasantries before leaving them again. 

Rio handed the alcohol to Agatha. 

“Do you own this shithole or something?” 

“No, god no,” Rio laughed. “I was their architect. The place used to be an indoor fish market. Yours truly made it into a cesspool for the baby gays.” 

Oh, right. Rio had actually managed to make a career out of that. Fair play. 

“At least you're honest about your work.”

“I’m honest about everything, Agatha.” 

Their eyes met with an intensity that Agatha would be embarrassed to admit it unnerved her. She had seen far too much of Rio Vidal recently for her liking.

“Is that right? Answer me this, then. What cunning plan has that little mind of yours conjured up this evening then? You knew I'd be here? Thought a bottle of bourbon would soften me up enough?”

Rio laughed under her breath. “I think we both knew as little about tonight as the other did. I’m here to support Alice, she’s taken a break from gigging recently. I also was as surprised as you were to find Jen there. Seems we’ve both been kept in the dark.” 

“Doesn’t explain the bourbon.”

“It doesn’t take a genius to see you were itching out of your skin to get out of here.”

“So you thought I’d come back with you instead? That is a bit presumptuous, Rio. Even by your standards.” 

“The only person talking about coming back with me is you,” Rio retorted. “I’d go as far to say the idea is pretty cemented in your head.” 

Fuck it. 

“Perhaps it is.” 

“Then it’s settled. I’ll get us a cab,” Rio inhaled the last drag of her cigarette before stubbing it out on the sidewalk. She turned back round to face Agatha. “Oh. We’re not sleeping together.” 

“Excuse me?” 

“I don’t need a pity fuck. You made that clear enough three weeks ago,” Rio said as if it was the most normal thing in the world. “Come back. I have some pot, if that’s still your thing. And you now don’t have to worry about my shitty cheap liquor as you so lovingly put it. Besides, I was looking for an excuse to leave as soon as I walked in, and it seems both our dates for the evening were only ever there for each other.”

“Fine. I’m there for the pot, not for you.”

Rio’s smile made her knees slightly weak. “Good. I'd expect no less.”

The ride to Rio’s apartment had been worlds apart from the one they shared three weeks before. Touches had been replaced by distance, kisses replaced by Rio quietly offering conversation and Agatha refusing to entertain it at all. Agatha had even decided to keep the bourbon unopened, Rio promising her a glass that she had not bothered with the first time. 

Agatha unlocked her phone, remembering she should at least let Jen know she would not be returning to witness her rendezvous with Alice. 

Agatha: Gone home. Text if you need me (please don’t). Enjoy your fuck with DJ desperate. 

Jen’s reply came surprisingly fast. 

Jen: Enjoy yours too. 

Agatha: She’s not a DJ. And we’re not fucking. 

Jen: But she’s desperate?

Agatha could not withhold the snigger under her breath. 

Agatha: Obviously, it’s me.

Rio looked at her with a raised brow. “Do I even want to know?” 

“No. Don’t want to hurt that pretty little ego of yours.” 

Their taxi pulled up on the sidewalk outside Rio’s building. The streets were quiet despite still being several hours until midnight. Agatha wished she had joined them in not leaving the comfort of their own homes, despite how little comfort her own offered. 

Rio paid their driver, opening Agatha’s door and offering her a hand. Their eyes met and Agatha swallowed as the warmth of hazel seeped into her. She considered slapping the gesture away, but surprised even herself when she took it. Rio’s hand was warm and right; a second wave of something deeply unsettling passed through her stomach. 

“Why is your lockscreen a rabbit?” 

Of course, Rio would wait to ask that until they were trapped in the four steel walls of the elevator. Agatha felt her cheeks redden slightly, eyes finding the floor as she gripped the rail behind her. Fuck sake this was a mistake, Agatha thought through a deep breath. 

She looked back up, rolling her shoulders back. “It was a drawing from a friend.” 

“Cute.” 

Please, don't. 

As if Rio understood her, they remained silent for the rest of their journey up the flights of the building. It felt odd to enter Rio’s apartment after only being there several weeks before. It felt even odder considering just how sober they both were - at least, in comparison to the last time. They hadn’t seen each other in two years, and suddenly it was the second time that month. 

“Make yourself comfortable,” Rio spoke as she moved towards the kitchenette. “You sure you want that glass this time?” 

“I thought you said fucking is off the table.” 

“No one said anything about a table-”

‘Yes, I want the glass.” Agatha rolled her eyes as she moved with Rio through the room, perching at the stool of her breakfast bar that had scattered blueprints littering it. “What’s these?” 

“Got a new project starting in the new year. Office building sold for an apartment complex.”

“Sounds riveting.” 

Rio shrugged her shoulders before reaching into a cabinet and retrieving two glass tumblers. She removed her black overcoat, revealing an oversized blue and white striped shirt that had a dangerous amount of buttons undone, and placed the coat on the back of the chair next to Agatha. 

Agatha tried not to watch her; tried not to notice the skin of her collarbone or the way her clipped-back hair revealed several piercings and a tattoo at the base of her ear. She did not remember that one from their previous encounter, but she did remember Rio having an affinity with ink-stained skin. When Rio handed her the glass, Agatha had to refrain from drinking it all in one go. 

“I’m going to roll. And change. Can’t be smoking in anything but sweats. You want some?” 

She could not help the way her entire body tightened at the thought. It was wrong; imagining Rio being so comfy around her was wrong, and imagining herself even allowing to get so comfortable around Rio went against anything she had ever stood for. So she shook her head and sipped at her drink, thankful for the burn against her throat. 

Whilst Rio had left for her bedroom, Agatha took her drink to the living area. She purposely chose the couch they had not ravaged each other on, and placed her glass beside it. 

Before sitting down, she wandered over to the bookshelf, her intrigue getting the better of her. She vaguely remembered looking at the framed photos in her drunken haze a few weeks before, but would never have been able to recall what she saw. She inspected the books Rio had and was pleasantly surprised to find several literary classics amongst the anthologies of architecture theory and giant catalogues on Bauhaus or Gaudi. 

It was one photo that piqued her interest however. It was clearly Rio, but younger than Agatha had ever known her. She must have been barely a teenager, grinning up at the camera with that endearing gap in her teeth on full display. Agatha didn’t know if she had ever seen Rio smile like that, despite the considerable -  and intimate - time they had spent together over the course of a decade. In the photo, an older woman and older man had their arms wrapped around her. It looked like they were on holiday, backpacks and shorts, all too happy for their own good. It was then that Agatha realised that she had never asked Rio about her parents. 

“Ah, don’t make me talk about them. You’ll really regret coming over here then.” 

Agatha turned to find Rio waiting near the hallway, her black slacks swapped for gray sweatpants. Thankfully, the shirt had not been replaced. 

She put the photo back on the shelf before offering Rio a small smile against her best nature. The air around them seemed thick and quiet, so much left unspoken. Both knew neither would enquire further, which was probably why they kept ending up in this situation. 

Without warning, Rio threw something big, soft and gray onto the seat beside Agatha’s drink. “Put it on, you look awfully stiff in that jacket.” 

“Charming,” Agatha remarked as she picked up the hoodie to inspect further. It was perfectly overwashed, oversized and she could already smell that musky earth that was entirely Rio. It was a disaster waiting to happen, but she ditched her pinstriped blazer regardless. 

Rio looked at her, tongue poking against her cheek as her head shook slightly. “On second thoughts, that was a bad idea.” 

“Oh?” Agatha quirked her brow, hands finding the pockets of the hoodie. “I like it.”

“I do too,” Rio’s voice softened before she clapped her hands together and retrieved the joint from behind her ear. “And that’s where the problem lies. Come on.” 

Rio picked up a large blanket with fraying yellow and orange thread throughout before heading to the french doors of her balcony. Agatha should have known to expect the botanical garden to continue outside, but she hadn’t expected the space to look like a small haven. There were multicoloured fairylights across the railings and overhead; a sofa made from old pallets was in the corner, complete with rug and coffee table, whilst smaller warm lights and candles allowed the ivy crawling up the wall to glow. 

“My happy place,” Rio mentioned as she sat down against the cushions. When Agatha looked around for an alternative option of seating, Rio simply tapped the space behind her. “Sorry sunshine, you’re stuck with me.” 

Agatha huffed, but moved towards her anyway. If being inches from Rio was not already enough, the way the woman placed the blanket over both of them had her considering jumping from the balcony altogether. 

They both sipped at their drinks, distant horns and clattering of bins heard from below. Rio lit the joint and took a long drag. The smell offered an immediate comfort to Agatha, knowing she soon would be blissfully unaware for at least a few hours of the night. 

“I read your piece the other day.”

“You and everyone’s mother.” 

Rio laughed softly as she exhaled, passing the joint to Agatha. “It was good. I mean, I knew you could write, but you can really write.” 

“They don’t pay me the big bucks for nothing, darling.” 

When she took a toke of the joint, the haze overcame her almost instantly. She allowed her shoulders to relax against the cushions, her legs joining Rio’s against the coffee table. She hadn’t expected this to feel so nice; to feel so normal. 

“Would you ever write something of your own?” Rio asked, head turning to face the woman beside her. Her voice was softer now - everything was softer now. Agatha could kid herself into thinking it could happen again. 

She shrugged her shoulders, avoiding Rio’s eye and instead finding the skyline in the distance. “That, unfortunately, does not pay the big bucks.” 

“I’m sure you’d have enough people that want to listen. You have a weird way of doing that, even when you’re not trying,” Rio told her as she took the joint back. “Besides, me and you have a little something extra that the average person can only dream about. Write about that.” 

Agatha laughed, arms crossing against her chest. “I don’t think my usual readers would appreciate a fairytale about witches.” 

“You literally write about fraudsters. Literal criminals, Agatha. I don’t think magic is too hard to comprehend.” 

“You’d be surprised.” 

Their conversation flowed easily, and thankfully about nothing of importance. Rio told Agatha about her travels to Europe the summer before, describing buildings with such fascination that Agatha had to force herself to look interested. It was an odd realisation, that she was willingly letting someone talk about something she simply did not care about. Still, there was a first time for everything. They spoke more on Agatha’s job, about how pointless it was to be a witch in this day and age. They even dared to reminisce on that one vacation that the two of them had joined Jen and Alice on back in 2012. 

They finished their smoke, and Rio went back inside to grab the bourbon before returning and refilling their glasses. Before long, they had been outside for two hours, not that either of them had noticed. 

Agatha was mid-pour of her third glass, when she started to register the discomfort in her stomach and slight spinning behind her eyes. She put it down to the several drinks she had consumed after smoking, and took a few deep breaths. Thankfully, Rio did not notice. 

She continued to drink anyway, listening to Rio explain her theory on how long she thought Jen and Alice had actually been in contact for. By her estimate, it was over six months, which left a sour taste in Agatha’s mouth at the realisation that she had not been the only one sneaking around in her friendship. 

When Agatha could not suppress her yawn, Rio looked up and offered a sincere curl of her lip. “Let me call you a cab.” 

Agatha wanted to protest, but she could not deny how exhausted she was. And perhaps, slightly disappointed that Rio had been true to her word about nothing happening between them that night. 

She agreed, taking one more sip of her drink before standing slowly. She thanked her lucky stars that Rio had already gotten up and opened the balcony doors, for the room nearly did spin upon standing and she did not need an invite onto Rio’s couch that night without the promise of anything more. She didn’t do sleepovers, and she was not about to break that rule tonight. 

In the living room, Agatha went to remove Rio’s hoodie but stopped still when a hand found her arm. 

“Keep-”

“Don’t tell me to keep it, Rio.” 

That little glint of life was back in Rio's eyes, making it near-on impossible to want to disappoint her. “Please, you look comfy.”

“If you’re using this as an excuse to see me again, so help you-”

“I’m not,” she shook her head quickly. “But if you ever fancy it, your bourbon will still be here.” 

“Noted.” 

Agatha retrieved her blazer from the couch before heading towards the apartment door, Rio close at her heel. They stood before each other, and for the first time that Agatha could ever recall, the silence between them was awkward. 

“Thank you,” Agatha spoke quieter than Rio knew she was capable of. “It’s been nice.” 

“Nicer than watching Jen and Alice flirt all night?” 

Agatha laughed, nodding her head. “So much nicer.” 

More silence fell between them. 

“Well, get home safe,” Rio eventually spoke. 

Agatha cursed herself for nearly replying with a “you too”. Instead, she offered one more nod and a sincere smile that did not usually belong to her, before walking down the corridor towards the elevator. 

“Agatha?” Rio called from the doorway. Agatha turned around, looking at her expectantly. “Can I have your number?” 

She sighed. “I am not walking back to give you my number, Rio.” 

Rio seemed to relax slightly, her lips curling into a smirk. “Come on, you know you want to.” 

Agatha hesitated. 

She had two options: turn on her heel and hope that these two alarmingly close encounters with Rio had been a fluke and she would never have to see her again, or walk back up that hallway and go against every moral within her to let Rio know she did want to see her again. 

She clearly had no morals, because her boots were walking her back up that hallway before she had even consciously decided to do so. 

The look of satisfaction on Rio’s face made her instantly regret it. 

“I thought you had my number-” Agatha began as she reached the doorway once more, before Rio sighed and cut her off. 

“You blocked me.” 

“Oh,” she held back a sheepish smile before passing over her phone, now onto an empty contact page. “If you so much as text me more than once a month, you’re getting blocked again. And no phone calls.” 

“Yes ma’am,” Rio offered a salute as she typed in her number and passed her phone back. “Have a good night, Agatha.” 

Agatha rolled her eyes, and turned once more to leave. As she began to move, a hand gripped the sleeve of the hoodie she wore, turning her back around. She was flush against Rio before she even realised what was happening. 

“What?” Agatha spoke, straight-faced despite the sudden closeness. “You love keeping me hostage, don't you?” 

Rio didn’t respond. Her eyes searched Agatha's own whilst her hand never left the gray fabric of the hoodie. 

Agatha could feel the heat of Rio’s breath, could smell the bourbon and weed on her. She could see the tiny freckles upon her nose and how annoyingly perfect Rio’s skin was. 

“Can I kiss you?” she whispered, their faces now inches from one another. 

Agatha just stared at her, breath caught in her throat. 

Rio took her lack of an answer as an answer, leaning in to allow just enough time for Agatha to retreat. When she didn’t, Rio’s lips found Agatha’s cheek, placing the softest, and torturously slow kiss there. 

“Really?” Agatha huffed, stepping backwards. She was all-too aware of the flush in her cheeks, the slight heaving of her chest. 

The woman before her simply smirked, retreating further into her doorway. 

“Goodnight, Agatha.”

 

 

One week had passed since her second evening spent with Rio. In that week, she had thankfully not heard from Rio once, but the nausea she had begun to feel had become a constant part of her days. 

She had tried to bury her head with work, had ignored Jen’s constant calls that demanded an update on what had happened between her and Rio. Agatha knew it was just a ploy for Jen to offload on her own escapades that night. 

She had told herself she was run down, that it was a stomach bug. Everyone was getting sick, anyway. It was that time of the year. 

Two weeks after that, she began to accept it was something she could no longer neglect. Her body was working against her in every way imaginable. She couldn’t keep down anything; the fatigue left her in bed for several days. Even her magic had begun to wane. 

And now? 

Now she wished her mother had gotten rid of her whilst she had the chance. Anything to not have to deal with this; anything to not be living this reality. 

The tiles of the bathroom floor were cold against her feet. Every inch of her seemed to be burning, and her heart may as well have been trying to punch a hole through her breast plate. She didn’t even know why she picked up the tests. 

The two lines that stared back at her were a cruel, cruel joke.

 

Chapter End Notes

Chapter 3

Chapter Summary

Agatha has a crisis, Jen is a good friend and we find out one of Agatha's biggest secrets.

Chapter Notes

TW for emetophobes in this chapter. oh, and we're getting soppy, but stick with it. it'll pick up soon and this should be the only chapter without agathario.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

It was a funny thing; Agatha Harkness was known to be a woman not to be messed with. She remained unshaken by anything. People of their kind whispered about her, feared her, avoided her. She could bring anyone - man, woman or witch - down to their knees. 

All it took to bring her to her knees was two pink lines on a stupid, pink, plastic stick. 

Two pink lines and she lost any semblance of control within her. 

She had no control left in her. She was at complete mercy to the body that was determined to work against her will in every way. All she could do was lift the toilet lid, with seconds to spare, and vomit. She did so repeatedly until her throat burned and her eyes were wet with tears. From exhaustion, or panic, she could not decipher. 

When her chest was left dry-heaving and there was physically nothing left to empty, her back slumped against the bathroom wall as she slammed her fist against plaster that was now splintered and her knuckles now bruised. 

“Fuck!” she screamed to no one in particular. 

Those two lines would be ingrained into her mind forever. 

How could she be so foolish? 

No, she hadn’t been foolish. She had slept with a woman. Well, women, plural. This wasn’t physically possible. 

The test had said otherwise. 

Shaking fingers reached back up to the counter where the test still sat. In the process of grabbing it, it slipped from her palm and fell onto the floor with a resounding slap. The sound echoed throughout the apartment, and her head too. She cursed under her breath and shuffled her body across the floor, arm outstretched. 

If anyone could see her now, she would probably laugh with them. 

The great, revered Agatha Harkness, crawling across a bathroom floor in her own puddle of sweat and tears. She had spent the last thirty seconds convincing herself that she was hallucinating. Her eyes were playing tricks on her. A witch with a vendetta against her had hexed her into a living nightmare. 

When she looked at the test once more, the second line had only gotten darker. 

She threw the test with her last remaining strength and cringed when it hit the large, circular mirror above the sink. The mirror cracked, a small shard falling against the marble counter. There was nothing left in her to react, so she laid her head back, body entirely spread out against the floor, chest heaving and mind spinning. 

She surprised herself when a hand rested against her stomach. 

“Those are your seven years of bad luck, kid,” she mumbled under her breath. 

Why was she speaking to it? 

There could not physically be a child inside of her. The test was wrong. A false positive. She had a stomach bug, even if it had plagued her for the last three weeks. It was physically impossible for there to be any other explanation. 

She tested twice more through a haze of pure delirium and dread. When both tests showed those same, taunting two lines, she vomited again. 

This cannot be happening, she told herself. 

Jen could help; Jen could give her an answer. Even if that meant telling Jen. Even if that meant making this - whatever this was - real. 

She realised she did not have many other options; if this was real, it would not go away, it would only become more noticeable. At least if she found out now, she still had time to do something about it. 

The thought had her throat heaving and head stuck down the toilet again. 

Time, she shook her head and laughed at the nauseating realisation. The one thing any mortal wished they could have more of, and I am cursed to have too much of it. 

Getting up from the bathroom floor was a task in itself; her limbs shook and she was sure her stomach would betray her again. The splash of water against her face was a small relief, the feeling of clean teeth ridding away the acid in her throat allowed her shoulders to ease from her neck slightly. 

In her room, she avoided her bed entirely. The temptation to curl under the covers and hide away from reality was only pushed away at the thought of sitting with this uncertainty a moment longer. 

She gave Jen the courtesy of changing from her vomit-ridden shirt to a black, ribbed set that helped ease the overwhelming suffocation from her now-discarded jeans.

Rio’s hoodie was still in her closet, still unwashed, still smelling of her. She stared at it for several seconds, her exhale shaky. Her throat burned, eyes becoming wet. She would never be able to return it to her. Not now. 

The worst part was: Agatha wanted the opportunity to return it to her. Not just to give it back, but to be able to see those warm eyes and those dimples and cheeky curl of her lips, just once more. She stashed that hope away with every other one she had ever had for her life.

She was taken from her thoughts at the sound of metal vibrating against porcelain; her phone was still in the bathroom and apparently the world was trying to interrupt her very-close to mid-life crisis. 

It had rung through twice by the time she gathered the courage to go back into that room. The mirror was still cracked, the three tests still discarded upon the sink. Her phone was close to falling to the floor as it began ringing for a third time. She was tempted to let it when she saw the contact name displayed on the screen. 

“What?” Agatha said as she answered the call, slow steps taking her back into her room where she eventually found purchase against the edge of her bed. 

“Darling, I haven’t heard from you in three days and that's how you greet me?” 

Agatha inhaled. 

“Now isn’t a good time, Lilia.”

“Is it work?” 

“No-”

“And you’re not dying?”

Agatha suppressed her laugh. She may as well have been. She wished she was, to be honest. Anything but this. 

“I will call you back. I’m running late for something.” 

“But I’ll still see you on Sunday? To put the tree up?” 

Her stomach dropped. The tree. That one, stupid, nauseating tradition that Lilia insisted they keep up despite it only offering them both pain over memories gone by. 

“Sharon - you remember Sharon? The lady from bridge club, the one that lost her husband a few months back and doesn’t stop talking, well anyway, in her copious amounts of spare time she decorated some new ornaments for us. But of course, they won’t replace-” 

Agatha could not hear anymore. 

“I’ll let you know. I need to go, Lilia.” 

“Agatha, don’t you dare hang up-” 

She ended the call anyway. 

Lilia called more times than Agatha could count; she ended up silencing her phone as she tried to make herself look semi-presentable before leaving her apartment.

She knew she would soon regret - or Lilia would make her regret - ignoring the one person in her life that had done everything for her. Lilia didn’t deserve that treatment. Despite the usual cold shoulder most people who came into contact with Agatha received, Lilia was the one exemption. The one person she could just be around.

Unfortunately, this situation did not provide her with the luxury of getting to just be. 

After throwing her hair into a claw-clip, she found her coat and keys and ordered a cab. She made sure to have extra cash on hand to throw at the driver in the likely event that she threw up in the back of his cab. Thankfully, despite the streets being traffic-ridden, the journey to the clinic took only twenty minutes and remained vomit-free. 

Getting from the cab to the waiting room was a blur, completely unregistered in her haze of need. The receptionist tried to get her information, tried to stop her from going any further, and Agatha simply shouted back: “if you want to keep your precious job, shut the fuck up!”

It didn’t take long to find Jen’s room; she had plastered the door in pink flowers and a glittery sign that read Jennifer Kale, OBGYN. The door opened before she even had a chance to knock. 

“Reception called, said some ‘crazed woman’ had broken and entered,” Jen looked at her as if it was just a regular Tuesday. She took in the woman before her, noticing her pale skin, shaking fingers and wide eyes. “I’ll tell security to stand down then. In you come.” 

When Jen opened the door, Agatha barely registered the office that surrounded her as she walked in. She saw the desk and the two chairs sat in front of it and decided she could not sit still. Instead, she paced from left to right with her palm against her forehead, hoping that Jen had miraculously learned how to read minds and she would not have to say the words out loud. 

Jen had, annoyingly, not learned how to read minds. 

Jen looked at Agatha as if she were to detonate any second.  “Do you want to sit down?” 

Agatha shook her head, continuing her pacing. 

“Do you want some water?” 

Agatha shook her head again. 

Before she could say anything, her body stilled, one hand flying to her mouth as she gagged into her palm. Jen moved quickly, grabbing the trashcan and shoving it in front of Agatha whilst she held onto her elbow and helped Agatha sit down on one of the chairs. 

Once she had emptied the content of her stomach for what felt like the one-hundredth time that day, Jen took the trash away from her, not without offering a gag herself.

The silence was thick between them. Agatha could not look Jen in the eye. Frankly, she regretted turning up at all. 

“Do I need to take you to the ER?” Jen’s words were tentative, her whole demeanour softening with her concern. Her hand rested on Agatha’s back, still stood but crouched beside her slightly. “Agatha?” 

When Agatha looked up, eyes red and wet, Jen took a step backwards, removing her hand. In all their decades of knowing each other, Jen had only seen that look on Agatha’s face once. Since that time, she had done everything in her power to never have to see it again. 

“You’re worrying me-”

Agatha cut her off, voice stronger than she felt. “I need you to do a test.” 

Jen’s eyes widened. “What?” 

Agatha groaned, wiping at her eyes. “A test, Jen. A blood test, I don’t know. Whatever it is you do. I need to know, immediately,” she took a breath, rolling her shoulders back. Jen continued to look at her, doe-eyed. “For fucks sake, why else do you think I would turn up here?” 

Jen pulled the other chair out, and sat down herself. “Are you saying that…? No - I mean, really?”

She shut her eyes, taking another deep breath. “Don’t make me say it.” 

“Ok, ok.” Agatha didn’t know which one of them Jen was trying to reassure. “How far along are you?” 

“I’m not anywhere ‘along’ until I know it is real.” 

“Have you done a test?” 

Agatha stared at her blankly. 

“Well, have you?” 

She nodded once. 

“And?” 

“Why else would I be here, Jen? Jesus - fuck - you’re supposed to be Miss Midwife over-”

“OBGYN,” Jen cut in. 

Agatha scowled. “I’ll vomit on your shoes next time.” 

Jen stood up, choosing to ignore the threat. She began walking over to the cabinets behind her desk, opening drawers and pulling out several neatly packed instruments and labels. “So, you got a positive. It could just be a false-” 

“Three.” 

“What?” 

“Three positives.” 

Jen tried to conceal her sharp inhale, but even through her own panic, Agatha noticed. 

“I can do your bloods, and the clinic will process them, but that’s still going to take a few hours.” 

“A few hours!” 

“I can do an ultrasound, but it wouldn’t be a normal-”

Agatha held her hand up, her body backing against the chair as if it could offer escape. “No. No. You are not going anywhere near there.” 

“I can get a colleague to-”

“No, Jen.”

Jen’s face softened, looking at her best friend despite said best friend avoiding her eyes entirely. “Agatha, did you not have to do this last time?”

“Don’t you dare bring that up,” Agatha spat, her expression leaving no room for argument. She clenched her jaw so hard she began to taste blood. No matter how hard she tried to keep them at bay, the tears began to stream down her cheeks. 

God, this cannot be happening, she thought to herself. She had been waiting for Jen to say it. Waiting for someone other than herself to bring up the obvious. 

“I can’t do this, Jen,” she eventually whispered, before covering her face with both hands. 

For the first time, she didn’t freeze when Jen wrapped her arms around her.

 

 

She did not know how long she had been at the Women’s Health Center, but it was long enough for her nausea to settle and be replaced with a deep ache in her stomach and what felt like something permanently lodged in her throat. 

Her bloods had been taken and sent off to the laboratory after Jen had a very strong word with the technicians that this was absolute top priority. She then forced her assistant to cancel the rest of her day without another word on the subject. 

Agatha had not processed that they had left, nor that she was forced into the back of a cab, or that they had arrived at the stoop of her building. She barely was present for the journey in the elevator, or when Jen asked her three times about the location of her front-door keys. 

“God, Agatha. Did your cleaner not show this week?” 

“No.” 

“Why?”

“I fired her.” 

Jen chose not to enquire any further. Instead, she began moving around the kitchen, putting crockery into the dishwasher and lighting a candle on the breakfast bar. 

“No, no, no. Blow that out, right now.” 

“What?” 

Agatha only had to dry-heave for Jen to rush to the counter and cease the candle from burning. “Ok, so the baby doesn’t like geranium or rose. Interesting taste.” 

“There is no baby.” 

Jen moved back to the couch, pulling a blanket over her best friend who now lay, unironically, in a foetal position. “Agatha,” she sighed. “You need to prepare yourself for your bloods to come back… if the three tests and vomiting all over my office wasn’t enough, the aversion to that candle says it all.” 

Agatha spat out a laugh. “Maybe I just don’t like that candle.” 

“Your ability to be delusional is admirable but half the wax has already been melted away.” 

Agatha mimicked her friend's words back before curling back up, bringing the blanket closer to her chin. “You don’t have to stay.” 

Jen shook her head, a soft chuckle escaping her. “Yes I do. I’m not being responsible for whatever it is you’re going to do when you receive those results.” 

Jen expected Agatha to meet her with a fight - with sarcasm, or anything Agatha. She did not expect the silence and searching of blue eyes into brown. Her best friend's voice was smaller than she had ever heard it before. 

“You really think…?”

Jen frowned and offered her a slow nod. 

“Its not possible, Jen.” 

“Why not?”

Agatha laughed through hot tears that had appeared without her consent. “You really think I slept with a man?” 

“You’re a witch, Agatha. Surely we know better than anyone that anything is possible.”

She hated how right Jen was. There were barely enough of them - of witches - to even know what was possible. Hell, most witches did not even know the extent of Agatha’s own powers. Jen certainly did not. Most witches would not be able to comprehend her capabilities, let alone those with a mortal mind. Which was why she kept her head down, and avoided ever being put in a situation where they may be found out. 

She was stolen from her thoughts at the sound of Jen gasping. She opened one eye against her tiring will, already preparing herself for whatever else utter shit this day was going to throw at her. 

“Oh my god. You and Rio, that night at Alice’s event. Did you-” The cogs turning in her mind were too loud for Agatha. She could only huff and pull the blanket over her face. “Oh my god. You did, didn’t you?” 

Agatha eventually sighed, unwillingly unearthing herself from her cover.  “Not that night.” 

“But it did happen?” Jen’s eyes widened. “You’ve been seeing her since then?” 

Agatha shook her head, teeth biting into her lip. “No.” 

Jen’s shoulders dropped slightly. 

“It was before.”

“Shut up!” 

Agatha’s palm found her forehead - still clammy, still too cold. “You hardly have a leg to stand on. When were you going to tell me about Gulliver?” 

“I’m not the one knocked-up right now.” 

“If I wasn't about to throw up my guts, I’d come over there and kick you. I hope you know that,” Agatha mumbled as she stared up at the ceiling. “I’m sorry to burst your bubble, but she’s not running around with a cock so, news just in, I’m the new fucking Virgin Mary.” 

Jen couldn’t hold back her laugh; Agatha couldn't blame her on this occasion. Agatha Harkness and The Virgin Mary just did not belong in the same sentence with one another. 

“So, there was a man? Come on, Agatha. No judgement here. We know you’ve been there before.”

She really, really wished she was in a position to ring Jen’s neck until she ceased to talk altogether. “Regardless of whatever questionable moments I’ve had in the past, I can assure you there are no men in the picture. Nor will there ever be, so wipe that smirk off your face and think of another fantasy to go home to tonight.” 

“When did you last sleep with someone?” 

“Jesus christ-” 

Jen feigned a gasp. “That is blasphemous, Mary.” 

Agatha found the strength to launch a cushion directly at Jen’s face. 

Jen took the cushion to the face with a grace that was ever-so Jennifer Kale. She offered a wry smile before continuing. “Well, when?” 

“Six weeks ago.” 

“With who?” 

“Rio.” 

“And before that?” 

“No one," Agatha admitted. "For two months.”

Jen had done the decent thing and decided she would shut up after that. Agatha’s head ached, her throat still scratchy and limbs heavy with exhaustion that was threatening to pull her under. Her best friend had eventually gotten up and continued to clean the room, insisting Agatha should try to sleep whilst they waited. 

She didn’t know how long she had passed out for, but was roused by a soft tug at her arm. 

“Agatha?” Jen’s voice was soft, lulling her into the false pretence that the events of that day had been simply a figment of her imagination. 

She couldn’t have been more wrong. 

She realised that even her eyelids ached as she tried to open them. Jen was now crouched before her, and the look behind her eyes was confirmation enough that she was about to find out the one thing their entire day had become centred around. 

Jen held her phone out, offering it to Agatha’s shaky hand. She got up, backing away onto the opposite couch as Agatha took the phone and felt her stomach threaten to leave her throat. 

Jen offered a small smile. “Looks like you and Rio need to talk.” 

She was cold already, but nowhere near the chill that had just run through her body. 

The phone screen displayed a PDF document, with too much technical jargon that Agatha did not intend to decipher at that moment. She didn’t need to. There was one, small word at the bottom that was outlined in bold. 

Pregnant. 

Jen disappeared. The traffic arguing with one another through her windows stopped. Even the beating of her heart had ceased. All she could focus on was that one, simple world. 

It was anything but simple to her. 

She did what she always had done best. She locked away any emotion from her expression, took a deep breath, shook the hair from her shoulders and handed the phone back to Jen. 

“I need you to leave.” 

Jen tried to protest, but Agatha held her hand up to quieten her. 

“Leave.”

“Agatha, I-”

“I am not going to do anything stupid, apart from sit here and contemplate why the fuck my life is the way it is, and what the fuck I am going to do about it. So, get out.” 

Thankfully, Jen knew not to test her patience anymore.

 

 

It had been enough time for the sunlight to disappear from the windows, replacing the room with a darkness that was oddly comforting. Agatha had not moved since Jen had left. She needed the toilet, she needed a drink and her stomach was practically begging her to nourish it with something. 

She couldn’t bear the thought of movement, just like she couldn’t bear the thought of looking after herself. Of looking after it. 

There was only one thing she wanted, but even that felt like she was undeserving of it. 

She found the strength to get up anyway. 

Instead of crawling under the covers of her bed like she knew she should do, she moved towards her closet. Her fingers shook, knees wobbling as she opened the doors. Rio’s hoodie was the first thing that caught her eye, but she couldn’t look at it for longer than a second. There was something else more important waiting for her. 

Closing the closet door on herself was an old, trusted blanket for her sadness. It knew her better than anybody else, and it offered more to her than any living thing could. 

She sat down in the darkness, eyes closed, and finally exhaled.

 

 

She stepped into the familiarity of her old bedroom, in her old apartment. 

Everything was the same, just as the tightening in Agatha’s chest was the same. It was a beautiful apartment - a beautiful home for them both. How it was always supposed to be. 

The sun was setting through the curtains, darkness slowly creeping into the room. A stranger stared back at Agatha when she reached the mirror. It was always a surprise to her - staring at a ghost of herself. The person she used to be. A person who no longer existed. 

Her face was softer, her features gentle. Her hair was slightly shorter, resting just below her shoulder blades. She was clothed in bootleg jeans and a blue sweater - his favourite one. 

His bedroom was opposite her own, just across the hallway. It never took long to get there - she could never bear to be far from her son. 

As she crossed the hallway, she could hear a soft mumbling of noise accompanied by an intermittent robotic voice crackling through plastic. Leaning against the doorway, Agatha felt claws around her throat. It never, ever got easier to see her son, despite seeing him everyday that it was bearable enough to do so. 

It was him, but it wasn’t him. He was truly there, but he didn’t exist anymore. It was really happening, but only because it had already happened. 

Nicky was sitting on the circular rug on the floor, two superhero figurines in his hands as he re-enacted whatever make-believe scenario his gorgeous mind had created. Here, he was three, his dark hair unruly and eyes bright with life. 

It didn’t take long for him to notice her presence in the doorway, his little face looking up at her as a deep smile formed upon his lips. 

“Mama,” he held up a figurine to show her. “Spiderman’s leg backwards!”

Agatha laughed softly through the burning within her body. She stepped into the room, crouching down to Nicky’s level as he began to explain what his toys were doing. 

When it took Agatha a moment to realise Nicky was looking up at her expectantly, she watched his brow furrow. “Mama?” 

Suddenly, every hair on her arm felt like shards of glass splintering her skin. 

He always knew. 

She wished he didn’t have to. 

She nodded her head anyway - she could never lie to that beautiful little boy. “I missed you,” she whispered, bringing her hand up to his cheek and tracing the outline of his jaw - softly, slowly, imprinting his features into her mind once more. 

“You just made dinner,” he replied, nonchalantly. 

“Of course,” She replied softly. “I hope I didn’t burn anything.”

When Nicky giggled, her head began to spin. It was her favourite noise to ever grace her ears. “Mama burned fries!”

“Silly me, huh?” 

Nicky nodded in agreement, continuing to giggle. 

“It’s getting late, baby. Time for bed?” 

He nodded once more, a flicker of disappointment on his face. Of course he didn’t want to sleep, he had so much more of life to discover - or so he believed. 

So they had both believed. 

When Agatha stood herself up, in tandem with Nicky’s own movements, her breath stilled. He took her hand before she could move, and proceeded to hold both of his arms out in a silent gesture that she could read easily: “Please pick me up, Mama.”

She always would. 

Feeling her boy, in her arms, the first place he had breathed his first breath, had his first meal, spoke his first word - it was the only time Agatha ever felt whole. The final piece to the over-complicated puzzle that was her life. 

Losing that piece had been a catalyst; now every damn piece was scattered from her reach. 

“Somebody’s getting too big,” she murmured against his head as her arms cradled the boy against her hip. His head instantly found solace against her neck, his body moulded to her own, made for each other. 

She gently laid Nicky down against the green and blue sheets, pulling the comforter of cartoon cacti over his small body. She perched her body next to where he lay, both feet still planted on the floor until he took her hand once more, beckoning her closer. 

“Cuddle?” 

His requests were always so simple; he would never understand the power behind them. They were Agatha’s gravity, keeping her grounded, keeping her going. 

Agatha smiled, nodding her head as she shifted her body onto the bed, her legs beside his own, one arm wrapping around his shoulders, her hand instinctively playing with his hair. 

“Mama?” Nicky craned his neck upwards, to look at his mother. “Mama quiet today?”

Agatha’s chest tightened at his words. She couldn’t bear to tell him what she had spent the day processing. She promised she wouldn’t lie to him, but she would also never forgive herself for telling him that there could ever be anybody else but him for her. 

“I’m just tired, baby,” she murmured against his head. Her hands twirled around one of his dark locks, her voice beginning to hum an old lullaby she had spent so many sleepless nights singing to him in the past. He settled into her, his breath beginning to even out.

“Nicky?”

He looked up at her again, his eyes boring into her own. Those eyes - so deep, so dark, so inviting, flooded with vigour and a sleepy excitement. Those eyes were so different to her own, yet they were the most familiar thing she knew. 

“Are you happy,” she began, “it being just you and me?” 

She had never asked him before. 

Perhaps it was through her own selfishness, or merely down to her guarded nature, but knowing what she knew now… she had been given a small glimpse into something she had resigned herself from ever experiencing again, a long, long time ago. 

Nicky looked at her with those beautifully curious eyes, his brows furrowing briefly as he considered her words. “Mama makes me happy.”

That was a punch directly into her chest. 

“You don’t wish there was ever somebody else?” She took a deep breath. “You don’t get lonely?” 

Nicky contemplated her words in a way only a four year old boy could. “I like having you,” he paused, his eyes briefly lighting up, “and Grandma Lilia too!”

She laughed softly at his response. She wished she could tell Lilia what he had said, but that would only invite another lecture on the past that she was unwilling to listen to. 

Agatha had taken his hand into her own, their fingers entwined as Nicky fiddled with the hem of her jumper, like he always would when he felt safe. She placed a kiss on his head, mulling over his response as they sat in a comfortable silence. 

“You don’t wish you had a friend to play with?” 

“I have friends, Mama,” he giggled, as if she had asked the silliest question she could. “Tommy, and James, and Billy. We play dinosaurs. I’m the big one, the tyran-rex!” 

“Tyrannosaurs Rex baby,” she whispered, placing another kiss on his temple. 

He attempted to repeat what she had said, ending up in them both laughing at the sheer impossibility of it. It was too normal; everything about it hurt. 

“You don’t wish you had a friend to play with at home? When Mama’s busy working?” 

That was always her biggest regret: she had spent so much of their short time together with her head buried in matters that held no say in how long his heart got to beat for. 

“Like Billy has Tommy?” 

“Yeah, something like that.” 

Nicky did not reply immediately. 

She imagined all the things his mind was conjuring up off the back of the new idea; all the make-believe stories he would never get to recreate, all the shows he would never get to put on, or the songs his lungs would no longer have breath for. 

He eventually shook his head. “Boys are mean. I only want a sister.” 

Oh. 

He was open to the idea. 

“Only if they make you happy,” he replied, his voice too innocent, his words holding meaning greater than he could ever understand. “They’d have to love dinosaurs like me. And animals! And plants!” 

She couldn’t think about his words too deeply, feeling her eyes already beginning to betray her. 

“Of course, we could never settle for any less,” Agatha poked gently at her son’s stomach, eliciting another giggle, re-centering her mind back to the real reason why she was there. Her son. Not some hypothetical maybe. “What story do you want tonight?” 

“Mama!” Nicky rolled his eyes, sighing softly as his lips curled into a smile - like she had missed the most obvious thing in the world. “Dr Seuss, remember?” 

Agatha glanced at the bedside table, a book placed under the glow of his koala-shaped lamp. Her breath caught in her throat briefly - of course, they were reading that book. She remembered it so clearly, turning each page, Nicky humming through the rhymes, Agatha imagining her little boy’s future ahead of him, before he had no future at all. 

After regaining her composure, her hand gently fiddling with his loose strands of hair upon his forehead, she reached for the book beside her. “Silly Mama, huh?” Agatha mused, earning another giggle from her son. 

She found the marked page that they had last paused the story on, a dinosaur-shaped bookmark sticking up from the book. When she saw the first words, she wanted to scream. 

Instead, she shook away her blind-panic, and held onto Nicky tighter.

Chapter 5

Chapter Summary

It's Jen's annual Christmas Eve party.

It had been twelve years since Jen had walked into their apartment and proclaimed that she was done with being a child. Or that she wanted to be more ‘adult’; Agatha could not remember her precise words, only the complete party-zilla her flatmate turned into when she decided it was time they host a civilised festive get-together with their close friends. 

In the midst of barely clinging on to a decent grade for her postgrad, and interning for a paper in Brooklyn, a party was the last thing on her mind but the promise of getting drunk did lift her ears a little. Until Jen told her that it was strictly not to be a party, and instead a candle-lit dinner to begin the rest of their lives. She later found out it was just an excuse to show off to the girl she had started dating, but thank god said girl brought a friend who had snuck in a keg, two bottles of tequila and a gram of coke. 

Every year since, the parties had only turned more illustrious, and slightly less drug-fueled. White sheets draped over furniture and silver, light-up balloons were dotted in every corner. It made Agatha feel like she was in a mirror maze. Someone had clearly vomited fairy lights upon every surface and crevice, and of course, there was a balloon arch. Jen would not be caught dead without a balloon arch. She already had made her decision. She just couldn’t think about it yet.
