She wished it was Rio wrapped around her, and not her damn hoodie. 

Her stomach rolled with a discomfort she had become too used to in recent weeks. She placed her hand against it unconsciously, before collapsing onto her bed. 

The secret was out, in the worst way possible and now she was about to enter a new year that would change her life as she had known it. And she would be doing it alone. 

All she could think about was the look on Rio’s face; her doe-eyes and quivering lip; the small laugh she had emitted as if she still thought it was a joke and then her face morphing into a pained expression that Agatha never wanted to see again. 

She wanted to see Nicky - no, she needed to see Nicky - but she was hesitant. 

She had no idea what stress travelling back would put on her body. She had no knowledge or past experiences to go on and anyone in her family who could have helped her were long gone, or purposely kept their distance from her. 

There was Lilia, but Lilia would only tell her what she didn’t want to hear: that she needed to move on once and for all. That had never been an option. 

As bad as it was, the pregnancy had survived the last three months of her visiting Nicky almost every evening so she took herself back to her closet to see him once more.

Tuesday, 31st December 2015. 

He was nearly eight months old and the sweetest baby you could meet. Agatha was biased but she truly believed it; no other child could compare to her little man. 

She could hear him babbling when she walked out of the bedroom and down the hallway in her old apartment. It was smaller than the one she lived in now. It was comical, at present, that she had more space but less life to fill it. 

His nursery was painted the same green that carried him through his life. The walls had prints hung up in frames: one of her twenty-week ultrasound, one of his small hand and footprints and another was a tapestry with an embroidered giraffe and his name spelled out below it, courtesy of Lilia. 

There was a box still unpacked in the corner; they had only moved out of Lilia’s home the month before and Agatha remembered ensuring Nicky had his space ready before the rest of the apartment was touched. A rocking chair sat near the window which left her smiling fondly at the memories of her son in her arms, suckling and whining as she comforted him. 

In the middle of the far wall, was a crib with an infant gripping at its bars, offering her a gummy smile. Her hand found her chest as she was met by the sight. 

“There’s my baby,” she whispered, walking across the room and crouching to the floor so she was level with him. 

Her fingers poked through the bars, tickling his chin, his chest, behind his ear. He giggled and swatted her away. 

“Mama missed you,” she said as she stood back up and leaned over the crib to gather him into her arms. 

“Mamamama,” Nicky babbled. 

The sound brought a warmth to her that she had been searching for ever since he had left. 

She realised only one thing had ever given her that warmth again.

Rio. 

“Are you going to let Mama give you cuddles before you scream?” she asked as she sat them both down into the rocking chair. 

Nicky looked at her for a few seconds before letting out a squeal, one of his hands clumsily patting her cheek. 

“Thank you,” she said as she caught his wrist. “That’s very kind.” 

He babbled more nonsense in response and she listened like it was of the utmost importance. Eventually, Nicky burrowed his head into her chest, curling his body into itself as he began to suck his thumb. 

“I don’t know what I’m doing,” she said softly, wiping hair away from his forehead. “I don’t want to replace you.” 

He couldn’t reply, but the way his eyes locked onto hers and watched every word leave her mouth told her that he understood the heaviness of it all. 

“I don’t want to replace you, and I don’t want them to feel like I can’t love them because I lost you,” she continued. “My mother was right, wasn’t she? I can’t have nice things; I hurt everything I touch.” 

He patted her cheek again, gentler this time. A tear escaped the corner of her eye. 

“You’d be eight years old now. I wish I got to see what that looked like. I wish I got to hear you getting grumpy about having to share me, and holding you even though you’d think you’re too old for it.” 

She would have taken a grumpy child over the ordeal she had just endured that night. 

“I wonder if you would have wanted a sister or a brother? When you were three, you said you only liked girls because you saw how your friends fought their brothers. Even at that age, you just wanted everyone to love each other. I wish I knew where you got that from.” 

Nicky lifted his head, looking into his mother’s eyes as if she carried all the secrets of the world. 

“I wish you got to meet Rio,” she wiped her cheeks before leaning her face against his hair. “I think she would have made us really happy.” 

“Now I’ve got no Rio, and no you,” she whispered. “Another year further away from you. I wish you could know how much I hate it. How much I wish I could go back and change it…” 

He patted her cheek again; another tear escaped her eyes. 

“... I tried. Believe me, I tried,” she said, looking into his eyes as she blinked away the tears. “Every single way. I think it took me a year before Lilia worked out what I was doing. Turns out some things are just written into time and there is no way of stopping them.” 

Nicky squealed, grabbing a strand of her hair and pulling at it. She barely reacted; the pain was minimal and she would endure it for however long it was needed if it meant she got to hold her son in her arms again.  

“... I just wish it didn’t have to be you.” 

“Mamamama,” Nicky began to babble again as he played with her hair. He began jumping up and down in her arms, continuing his chants with a glee that told her he was none the wiser to her emotions in that moment. 

“You want to go play?” 

Nicky giggled, bouncing with more tenacity. 

She lifted herself off the rocking chair with Nicky firmly held against her hip before carrying him through to the living room. The morning light streamed through the curtains, a reminder of snow still visible on the distant sidewalks. 

Agatha placed Nicky upon the rug in the centre of the room and brought his box of toys over. She sat beside him as they began to empty the toys; she watched him as he played and allowed herself a brief reprieve from the reality she was still to return to. 

It felt like she had been there for hours by the time Nicky started to become disgruntled and objecting to each new toy she offered him. 

“You’re getting grumpy,” she noted as she gathered him into her lap. Nicky protested the movement, despite curling into her chest the moment he felt her warmth. “Mama put you down for a nap?” 

Of course, he didn’t reply but he burrowed his head further against her shirt. 

She lifted him back up into her arms and carried him back to his nursery. She sang to him, stroked his hair and pressed dozens of kisses against his temple. 

“Happy New Year, baby. Mama loves you.”

 

 

Regardless of how long she had been gone, there were still forty-five agonising minutes until they said goodbye to another year. That was the problem with her so-called gift: there was no going forward, only back. 

That meant forty-five minutes of trying to distract herself from the impending doom that the new year brought: loneliness, motherhood and longing for a life that couldn’t exist. 

It was only when she left her bedroom that she heard the knock at her front door. 

She groaned to herself, hoping it wasn’t Lilia, or Jen, or anyone for that fact. She actually hoped she was going insane and hallucinating things. At least that would have been a more digestible confrontation. 

Approaching the door, she couldn’t help but curse the fact that she did not have a peephole. 

What apartment door in New York did not have a peephole? 

The need for one had never really arisen before; Lilia had the spare key, Jen would call her fifty times to remind her she was coming over and Agatha had not let another soul into her space. 

Apart from the maintenance man, that was. 

She opened the door slowly; only the smallest amount before it was restricted by the latch. 

The moment she saw Rio, blood rushed to her ears; her face; her chest. 

“At least I know I got the right number,” Rio said through the small crack. 

Agatha’s heart thudded. “Nope.” 

She slammed the door shut again. 

“Oh, come on Agatha!” 

“Go away,” 

“No.” 

“Why are you even here?” 

“Because we need to talk,” Rio called back through the door. 

“Well, you’ll be talking to the door. Goodnight.” 

Except she found herself being unable to leave. 

The reality of the situation, of that night, hit her and all she could do was slide her back down against the door and sit on the floor with her knees curled up against her chest. 

“Agatha?” 

She didn’t respond. 

“Are you still there?” 

She heard Rio sigh, and felt the door push slightly into her back. 

Rio had sat down as well.

“Look if you’re there…” Rio paused, Agatha clutched her knees tighter. “... I’m not leaving until you open this door. I’ll wait until next year if I have to.” 

“That was a shit joke,” Agatha announced her presence, a small smile appearing across her face despite herself. 

She heard Rio laugh softly. 

“I’m sorry tonight ended up the way it did,” Rio spoke. 

“You have nothing to apologise for. This is all my fault.” 

“No it’s not,” she laughed again. “Yes, you’re the one in this position out of the two of us, but it doesn’t mean it is your fault. Plus, you were right. Ralph is an absolute asshole.”

Agatha remained silent, listening to the words meet her ear as her eyes hazed over. 

“I… I understand why you kept it from me. I do.” 

A tear escaped, rolling down her cheek. 

“But you don’t have to deal with this alone, anymore.” 

She wanted to retort, she wanted to shout back. She wanted Rio to know that she couldn’t trust that; that she was always alone no matter who was in her corner. 

“Are you going to let me in?” Rio asked after a period of silence, each of them catching their breaths. “I understand you don’t want to. I understand this is your space, but I don’t want you to be alone right now.”

She continued after another pause. “Honestly? I want to be with you and no one else. The moment you left my apartment, it felt wrong.” 

She couldn’t stop the steady stream of tears now, each one a betrayal of the person she was supposed to be.

“No,” she whispered. 

“Let me in.” 

“No.”

“Agatha,” Rio sighed. “Come on, we have half an hour until the new year. Let me in.” 

“No.” 

“What are you so scared of?” 

If she let Rio through that door, there was no going back. 

Her space was sacred, no matter how impersonal it was to her. It was the one place that offered her a chance to be herself with no mask, no bite and no control. 

Letting someone into that was an irrational fear she had never been able to overcome in the wake of losing her son. 

“... Of everything becoming real.” 

“It’s already real.” 

Agatha stood up slowly, unlatched the door and opened it even slower. 

The moment their eyes met, another tear escaped. 

“I know,” she whispered. “That’s what I’m scared of.” 

She didn’t expect Rio to wrap her arms around her, and she didn’t expect to melt into the embrace but Rio’s arms were stronger and surer than Agatha felt in that moment, and that was enough for now.

“You can let go now,” Agatha said into Rio’s hair after they had been stood their for more than a minute. 

Rio groaned and held on tighter. “I don’t want to.” 

Agatha's laugh was muffled. “You're squashing the baby.” 

Rio jumped away from her like she had been burnt. Her eyes were wide and mouth was open as she looked Agatha up and down. “Are you ok? Are they ok? Oh my god-” 

Agatha laughed as she wiped her eyes. “I’m joking, Rio.” 

Seeing her so concerned settled something within Agatha; Rio cared, she really did. 

“Come in,” Agatha said as she stepped back and took her coat, discarded from earlier, from the back of the couch, moving to hang it up. “I wasn’t expecting guests so… excuse the mess.” 

Rio scoffed as she took in the surroundings. “You’re insane. I don’t think I can see a spec of dust.” 

It was true, the place was impeccably clean; impeccably un-lived in. 

“Do you want a drink?” Agatha asked as she moved to the kitchen, opening the cabinet and retrieving two glasses. “I have alcohol if you-” 

“No.” 

“No?” 

“If you aren’t drinking then neither am I.” 

She tried to hide her smile. “Ok, I have soda, juice, water-” 

“Agatha,” Rio interrupted from the couch. “Sit down.” 

Agatha looked at her. There was no chastising, or malice behind those hazel eyes, only concern. 

She filled the two glasses with water before moving towards the living area. Just as she was about to sit down on the couch opposite Rio, she felt a hand brush her waist. 

“Come here,” Rio said softly. 

Agatha sighed before placing the two glasses upon the coffee table and allowing Rio to pull her down onto the couch beside her. 

Rio’s hand didn’t leave her, it only moved to rest against her shoulder as she grabbed one of the tassels of her hoodie and started to play with it with her fingers. 

Agatha looked at Rio. She watched her as her eyes remained on the tassel and the movement of her fingers. She watched the way her brows knitted in concentration and her lips pursed together in thought. 

“How did you find me?” Agatha asked. 

“Jen.” 

“That bitch,” she muttered under her breath. 

“I like your hoodie,” Rio said with a smirk. “Also, since when did you wear glasses? And how do I get you to wear them every time I see you?” 

Agatha had forgotten she even had them on; she couldn’t even remember getting back into her apartment, she couldn't remember changing out of her outfit that had been wasted on such an awful evening and she certainly could not remember wiping away her makeup, taking out her contacts and pulling her hair into a messy ponytail. 

“Shut up,” she said through a small laugh, although there was no malice behind her words. “You’re party. You left. Rio-”

“It’s fine. They can keep themselves occupied.” 

“They’re in your apartment, alone?” 

“Alice will keep things in check,” she assured her. “Besides, you’re more important.” 

She pretended that didn’t make her swoon. 

“I don’t know what you want me to say,” Agatha admitted after a brief silence. 

“You can start by putting the TV on,” Rio looked around the living area, her nose scrunching and brows furrowing, “if you have one, that is. I need background noise.” 

“Ball drop?” 

Rio nodded. “Ball drop.” 

Agatha reached for the remote upon the coffee table. When she switched the television on, and the ostentatious artwork hanging above the fireplace illuminated, Rio turned to look at her with her mouth parted. 

“What the fuck, Agatha.” 

“It’s like magic,” Agatha teased. “You’ve not seen one of these before?” 

Rio shook her head as she laughed. “No, I have. I just-” 

Agatha waited for her to finish. 

Rio made a face. “Of course you have one.” 

Agatha set the channel to ABC and let the noise fill their background. 

She briefly looked at the large crowd, celebrating just a few miles down the road into a part of the city she would never dare venture into unless it were for her job. 

She saw the excitement on their faces; she saw their glee at the prospect of a new leaf about to be turned and wondered if any of them had already lost their heart as well. 

Despite her sad musings, she had to remember the positives. And they were that Rio was sitting right next to her. That Rio had left her New Year’s Eve party to be with Agatha. That Rio had chosen her. 

“So,” Rio began. 

“So,” Agatha echoed as she looked into her eyes. 

“I knocked you up.” 

There was a grin on Rio’s face that told Agatha she saw it as an accomplishment. 

“You know, it’s funny when people use that term and they are not carrying a literal child. When they are carrying a literal child, it starts to feel a bit…” 

“Ok,” Rio held her hands up. “I’m joking.” 

There were several beats of silence between them. 

“How do you know?” Agatha asked, her fingers wringing together in her lap. 

“Again, Jen. She gave me a pretty big earful actually. I believe several people heard me be referred to as your baby-daddy, despite none of them being witches, so that's going to be a fun one to explain at work next week.” 

Agatha grimaced at the thought. “Sorry you had to go through that.” 

“Don’t be,” Rio said softly. 

She reached for Agatha’s hand; the moment Agatha felt her touch, her breath caught. 

“I just need to know, before we start talking about this… there really was no one else?” 

Agatha shook her head slowly. “I’m sorry to disappoint.” 

“It’s far from disappointing,” Rio quickly told her. “I just-”

“Don’t believe me?” 

Rio looked at her; really looked at her. She tightened her grip onto Agatha’s hand. 

“Maybe.” 

“I can’t be angry at that. I’ve been a bitch.” 

“You have,” Rio answered, slightly too quickly for Agatha’s liking. “But I understand why now.” 

“You don’t know the half of it,” Agatha laughed. 

“True, but I want to. If you’ll let me be there with you.” 

Of course she had to say something as devastating as that. 

“I think I want you to be,” Agatha’s voice was small. 

Rio looked at her sincerely. 

The moment stretched thin and Agatha found herself imagining Rio in several months' time, sitting right where she was now, with a tiny human being nestled in the safety of her arms.

It was a terrifying thought that warmed her heart in a sickening way. 

“Wait here,” Agatha said as she moved to get up from the couch. Rio instinctively held onto her arm the moment she moved, looking up in question. “I want to show you something.” 

When she returned from her bedroom, she held out the photo in her hand. 

Rio took it, her brows knitted. When she flipped the scan over, her eyes widened. 

“Oh,” she said softly, eyes taking in every inch of the photo. “Oh my god. That’s them?” 

“That’s them.” 

“Our baby?” 

She couldn't believe she was sitting next to Rio Vidal, hearing the words ‘our baby’ leave her mouth. 

“Our baby,” Agatha repeated, her smile small.

“They’re beautiful.” 

Agatha laughed. “They’re a blob, Rio.” 

“A beautiful blob,” Rio smiled to herself as her thumb traced the image. “How far along are you?” 

“You can’t do the math?”

“Agatha, we’ve slept together several times in the last few months.” 

Agatha rolled her eyes. “I’m nearly eleven weeks; that was ten weeks along.” 

“So in summer, everything changes?” 

“For you. I have the misfortune of enduring watching my body turn against me before they even bother to show up,” Agatha scowled, but the moment was short-lived and far from the truth. 

Rio smiled, her eyes barely straying from the ultrasound image. 

“... Do you want this, Rio?” 

The words left her mouth before she had the chance to catch them. 

Rio’s eyes flicked from the scan to Agatha’s own. There was a certainty behind them that offered no hesitation; no doubt.

That was when the countdown to the new year began on the television. 

Ten seconds and she would be one year further from her son. 

Ten seconds, and she would reach the year she got to meet her baby. 

When the countdown got to the final five seconds, she felt Rio’s hand grip at the neck of her sweater. At seven seconds, Rio’s other hand found Agatha’s jaw and gently turned it to face her. When the cheers of celebration erupted on the screen, Rio’s lips had found her own. 

It was sweet and gentle whilst hungry and determined. 

She couldn’t help the whine that left her throat as Rio took her bottom lip into her mouth and sucked against it, her hand coming up to cup Agatha’s jaw and pull her closer. 

Everything she had endured that week was a distant memory. All she could focus on was the woman before her, and those lips and their tantalising movements against her mouth. 

When Rio pulled away, her eyes continued to search into Agatha’s own. 

“Does that answer your question?” 



Chapter End Notes

and finally we can all breathe

Chapter 8

Chapter Summary

Agatha and Rio have a much-needed talk (part one).

Chapter Notes

you so nearly got smut but i had to cut the chapter into two halves so instead you have babies being babies having babies and i will not be partaking in this much fluff ever again because it is not for me but hey we needed to do some character building (for said characters and for myself)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

In an unexpected turn of events, Agatha Harkness did not end up spending New Year's Eve alone. In fact, she spent it with Rio Vidal’s tongue down her throat. 

Even if she had to endure a night of humiliation to get there. 

It had been the first countdown where she hadn’t immediately wanted to off herself at the realisation that it was another year she had to get through without Nicky. Instead, she could only focus on the lips on her own, the hands fisting into her hair and the overwhelming urge to make sure she got to do this whenever she pleased. 

“Does that answer your question?” 

Rio’s voice had been dark, her eyes deep. 

Agatha could only seize her lips again. 

She wasn’t ready to confront everything now that she had this. 

It was only after hands began to slip under shirts and legs became entangled that Agatha pulled away. Both of their chests heaved; their eyes darkened; their lips swollen. 

“Words would be good,” Agatha said as she traced a nail along Rio’s jaw. “A lack of them is how we ended up here in the first place.” 

“...You want me to sign a contract or something?” Rio arched her brow but her lips remained curled. 

Agatha narrowed her eyes, her other hand poking a finger into Rio’s chest. 

Rio sighed. “Of course I want this, Agatha” she said, before smirking again. “Twenty-year-old me would faint if she could see me now. I knocked up Agatha Harkness… half the soccer team would be jealous of me.” 

Agatha frowned. “You’re still drunk.” 

“No I’m not,” Rio copied her frown. “This news was pretty sobering-” her eyes widened, “- in a good, happy, yay sobering way.” 

Agatha shoved her shoulder lightly as she got up from the couch. “You need food, come on.” 

She made her way into the adjoining kitchen and began opening cupboards only to survey what little she had stocked. “And drink your water too,” she called out, bent below the island out of sight. 

When she had retrieved a frying pan and placed it onto the cooker, she was met with Rio staring at her, wide-eyed and mouth open as if she were about to laugh. She had twisted her body so she sat facing the couch, a cushion clutched to her chest, watching the scene in front of her play out like it deserved to be on television. 

“What?” Agatha asked. 

“You are going to be an insanely hot mom.” 

Agatha’s stare hardened as she threw the spatula currently in hand, directly at Rio’s head. 

“Ow!” 

“Rio-” 

“Sorry, sorry. I can’t help it,” Rio said through her laughter. She picked up the spatula and her glass of water as she moved away from the couch to join Agatha. 

“What’s on offer?” Rio asked as she sat down on a kitchen stool. “You, I hope?” 

“I’m not on offer anymore.” 

“Anymore?” Rio’s eyes widened. “Please don’t tell me you’re doing this for the whole nine months-” 

A dish cloth was thrown at her next. 

“No,” Agatha hissed. “I meant this evening.” 

“I keep thinking about that skirt you wore tonight…” her eyes darkened as she watched Agatha move around the space, inspecting the fridge and very purposefully ignoring Rio. “...Or what I’d do to you in it.” 

She was looking at the outdated milk cartoon when she closed her eyes and took a deep breath.

Of course Rio’s words excited her. They excited her a worrying amount and she knew that if she acted on that excitement, the pair of them would be no closer in actually reaching a conclusion on what the hell they were doing with one another. 

Agatha clutched a hand to her heart, spinning round to face Rio. “Oh no,” she gasped. “I see no skirt anymore.” 

“I know you wore it for me,” Rio teased. “If I take you out on a date, will you wear it again? With those glasses on too, preferably. Actually, can you just wear those glasses everytime I see you? Contacts are a waste of money when you look that good without them.” 

Agatha’s grip on the knife she held tightened, biting back her blush with a hard stare. “You can have a grilled cheese, or you can leave.” 

Rio’s smile widened. “You wore it for me.” 

Agatha continued to ignore, busying herself by buttering the skillet and slicing cheese. If Rio wouldn’t appreciate her culinary prowess, which was severely limited in its capabilities, then she knew her own body would thank her for the snack. 

And it would be the only snack she was getting tonight, despite Rio’s obvious desperation and her own growing temptations.  

Rio ended up hailing Agatha’s grilled cheese as the best she had ever had, and subsequently collapsed onto the couch with a satisfied groan. 

When Agatha made a move to sit on the vacant couch, Rio glared at her. “Agatha!” 

“Shush,” Agatha chastised. “I’m right here.” 

“You should be here,” Rio indicated to the space beside her, lips pouting. 

There was something about Rio’s innocence, even if it was due to inebriation. It left Agatha feeling a little warmer to see Rio’s affection so brazenly displayed. It helped to push away the constant doubt that wanted to slowly seep in and take over her entire mind. 

So she accepted Rio’s demand, sitting down next to Rio with a blanket thrown over their legs. She didn’t even protest when Rio asked if they could watch The Lego Movie, even though it had been a favourite of her sons, because she knew the only thing she would be able to concentrate on was the woman beside her and the fact that maybe - maybe - they could make this work. 

It was only when her own eyes began to get weary, and some god-awful song began playing in the movie, that Agatha realised Rio’s breaths had evened out against her shoulder, her head having found purchase there only ten minutes after pressing play. She was peaceful, her arm strewn across Agatha’s stomach without even realising it. 

Her phone read 2.45am. 

It was too late to send Rio home. She couldn’t send Rio home; she didn’t want to send Rio home, but she had never had anyone sleep in her bed with her before. The thought left her throat a little too tight. She tried to focus on the soft breaths that intermittently warmed her neck and the one fact that remained true: Rio hadn’t left. 

Not yet.

And for the first time in her life, Agatha didn’t feel like she had to make her leave, either. 

So she gently roused Rio from her slumber and guided her into the bedroom. 

“Rio, let's go to bed,” she had whispered as she traced a finger along Rio’s jaw. 

It was a sentence she never thought she would get to say. 

“You’re letting me stay?” Rio then asked in a half-asleep mumble as Agatha eased her from the couch and through the apartment to her room. It was almost as if Rio understood the momentousness of Agatha’s decision, despite her drunken haze.

“Yeah,” she replied softly. “I’ll get you some clothes to sleep in.” 

Which was how she found herself, in her bed, accompanied by Rio Vidal who wore one of her old college t-shirts and a small pair of shorts that Rio had raised an eyebrow at and told her that, quite frankly, she just wanted to see Agatha wearing them. 

She was thankful for Rio’s slight inebriation; it afforded her less worry about Nicky’s blanket being unearthed from underneath her pillow and the subsequent onslaught of questions she would no doubt receive. Whilst Rio was in her bathroom, she had given herself a few minutes clutching it to her chest, breathing it in before it was hidden away in her closet. 

Agatha had thought she would be on edge with Rio in her bed but the moment the mattress sank slightly more than usual and an arm clutched over her hip, she realised it was a comfort that she could learn to let herself accept. Maybe even to need.

 


When she roused from sleep, she became aware of two things. She could hear soft snores sounding from behind her and, secondly, there was a weight pressing into her back and an arm around her waist. The usual fatigue that made her limbs heavy and head foggy hadn’t seemed to wake up with her that morning either. Light attempted to break through the curtains but it wasn’t harsh on her eyes like usual, instead it was welcomed accompaniment as she regained consciousness. 

Agatha lay, still, for several minutes as she remembered all that had happened the night before. The party; Ralph; Rio finding out about the pregnancy. 

Rio. 

That was whose fingers clutched at the material of her shirt. 

Her every movement was minute as she turned around. Rio remained peacefully, deeply asleep. Her features were relaxed, her mouth slightly parted and her hair making an escape from the bun she had put it in haphazardly the night before. 

One of her fingers gently traced the intricate pattern of leaves inked into her skin, just poking out from the collar of her shirt below her neck. Where the vine ended, the impressive silhouette of a tarantula began at the base of her earlobe. Agatha remembered the last time she had seen that tattoo, and how judgemental she had been about it. 

Little did she know, that spider was probably one of the only things that had enabled Rio to get so far in life; that had enabled her to keep going. 

That morning felt worlds away from the morning she had woken up at Rio’s just several weeks before. It felt right. 

But she couldn’t help but wonder, as she lay there watching the woman beside her, if she would ever get the chance to have a morning like this again. She couldn’t help but replay in her mind the words that needed to be said between them. She couldn’t help but doubt that they’d come out of that conversation even more splintered than they had been before. 

It was lovely, waking up beside Rio. She could breathe a little easier knowing she had broken down that final barrier and let Rio into her space; her world where she didn’t have to pretend. That one hard part was over. But with that barrier now broken, it left her armour wearing thin and her heart more vulnerable than she had ever allowed before. 

Agatha gently removed Rio’s hand from her waist and slipped from underneath the covers. She moved with a quiet precision that could only have been learned from months of raising a newborn that cried the moment they felt your touch leave them. She had never been able to shake that skill; it would always be a part of her, even if she had no use for it in recent years. 

When she returned with two coffees in hand, Rio had buried her head between the pillows. Agatha couldn’t help but smile to herself at the sight. 

“Morning lazy,” Agatha said softly as she got back into bed. 

A groan came from in-between the pillows. 

“I made you coffee.”

Once Rio got back, everything could change between them. 

No. When Rio got back, everything would change between them. 

Agatha could only hope it was for the better.

If a feather had decided to fall upon the floor at that moment, they both would have heard it.

Green witches were a myth. Green witches didn’t exist. 

Rio Vidal could not be one. 

There was no way. 

Agatha didn’t know enough about them to know for sure, but she knew from Lilia’s brief teachings that a green witch was a witch beholden to life itself. Why wouldn’t a witch so intrinsically linked to the beings of nature be able to produce the very thing its magic survived on?

Could Rio save Nicky?

Despite the myriad thoughts and feelings that could send her spiralling again, she could only focus on the warmth of arms around her, the smell of pine and musk and the cold touch of her fingers upon the scan photo. 

As she felt her eyes begin to fail her, she let herself do something she had not done in years: she let herself consider the future.

She imagined what life could be like three hundred and sixty five days further into learning one another. They could be in this very position, on this very bed, in this very same embrace, with a very new human of their own creation resting in their arms. 

She hoped, at least. 

There was one thing she knew for certain: she could not continue to screw things up with Rio like she had been doing.

Not now that she knew what they could have together. 

That there really was something there to build. 

“Rest,” Rio whispered. “I’m right here.” 

“But we didn’t talk-”

“We don’t need to talk about any of that right now,” Rio spoke softly as her fingers traced circles against her forehead. “We will. I don’t want you thinking I don’t care about what happened because I do, more than anything. But you’ve done enough this morning, Agatha.” 

“You’re not leaving?” Agatha's voice was so small. 

Rio smiled, leaning down to place a kiss against her temple. “I’m not leaving.” 

“Promise?”

“I promise.”

And with Rio's words and the way she looked down at Agatha like there was no one else worth looking at, it was a little easier to close her eyes.

Chapter 9

Chapter Summary

Agatha and Rio continue their talk. Oh, and other stuff... (peep the new tags).

TW - MENTIONS OF MISCARRIAGES

Chapter Notes

sorry for the two week delay, have fourteen thousand words to make up for it

See the end of the chapter for more notes

The air was too blue and the ground too green. She could feel the sunlight warming her bare shoulders whilst blades of grass brushed softly against her legs. Time moved differently to what she was used to. There was no linear, only flickers of moments, of brightness, of laughter. It didn’t feel like a memory; it felt lived. 

It was the laughter that she first became aware of, circling in her ears without being able to see it. Two different sets of laughter debuting a score she had never heard before, but knew she wanted on repeat. One was deep and mature in its softness. The other one more uncontrolled; more explosive in its innocence. 

Another flicker of light; another host of giggles. She turned her head to see a woman standing, blades of grass enveloping her silhouette and that of a small person - a young child - stretching up at her. An infant with dark, curling hair that reached the nape of their neck, whose legs wobbled and hands put their trust into the woman in front of them. 

She recognised the woman. She would recognise those hazel eyes in any lifetime. 

But she didn’t recognise the child. 

She couldn’t see their face. She wanted to, but with every movement her mind willed, her body remained still. 

“Rio?” 

It was her voice, but she didn’t feel it leave her mouth. It echoed in her ears like it belonged to somebody else. She tried to call out again but they just continued to laugh and dance and spin in the grass. Her fingers outstretched in the hope she could touch them and feel their happiness spread into her veins, but to no avail, she was sedentary. 

There was a pull in her chest, telling her to talk to the infant; to hold them; to laugh with them. She wanted to learn them. Was that her child? Was that their child?

She wondered if all the years spent abusing her travels back in time had finally caught up with her. She wondered if this was a cruel twist of fate; that she would be stuck in a foreign future rather than the past she yearned for. 

It was only when a new voice reached her ear that she realised this was a much crueler twist of fate. It wasn’t the future; it couldn't be, because that voice, calling her name, a name she hadn’t heard in over five years, could never have been in her future. 

“Mama!” 

She was dead. She had to be. Perhaps these were her final seven minutes; her life playing out before her. Everything she had, everything that could have been. 

“Nicky?” 

Again, it was not her own voice. It was a reverberation of muffled octaves that she was sure she had wanted to say, but her mouth had never opened. 

“Mama,” the voice came again, strangled by the distance. “Help!” 

That was when what was golden turned to darkness. The world around her was an expanse quickly closing in on her. The laughter still played on but it now lacked its warmth. Instead, it was a barrier between her and her son. The living blocking the dead. 

She had no clue where she was, or what had happened; she just knew she needed to get to Nicky. Each movement was forced, until the flashes and flickers returned, until she stood and her surroundings became more clear. In the distance, behind a veil barely visible to the eye, was a young boy. His small body was clawing at the veil, fists beating against something that was not there. Her name on his lips kept reaching her ears, over and over again. 

The laughter kept overpowering him. 

Agatha was crying out his name now. She was sure it was her. Someone shouted for her son and she hoped they would get to him if she couldn’t. She called for Rio, begging her to help, to stop, to listen, but hazel never turned her way. 

She was stuck. Her son needed her. Another child did not, and Rio wouldn’t listen to her. 

They just kept laughing. 

And Nicky kept watching, crying out her name. 

She couldn’t reach him. She couldn’t hold him. She couldn’t save him. All she could do was listen to his helpless cries for her and remember that she was a poor excuse for a mother. 

His cries drove into her chest, her ears, her entire being, even as they became more distant, even as another voice began to call for her.

“Agatha?” 

It was then that she realised the laughter had been replaced by a soft call of her name, repeated over and over again. There was no field or small child giggling in the arms of another. Nicky’s cries had faded until she was sure her mind was making them up. 

“Agatha? Come on, wake up.” 

Wake up. 

She hadn't died; she had dreamed. 

And now, after slowly blinking her eyes open, she found that she wasn’t outside; that there were walls and light escaping curtains and something heavy and warm enveloping her head. 

Her face was squished against something soft. There was weight against her shoulders; against her jaw. The monotonous tone of a voiceover on television played in the background as she came to her senses. 

“Hey,” the same voice as before spoke softly, A hand grazed at her chin, all the way up to the tip of her ear, repetitively, back and forth. “You’re ok.” 

It was Rio, she quickly realised. A different Rio from the one that had been in her head. It was Rio’s arms that wrapped around her body and it was Rio’s chest her head laid against. 

“What’s going on?” 

It was the only words Agatha could manage amidst the confusion in her brain. 

“You were asleep,” Rio said. “Are you ok? It sounded like…”

Rio’s words trailed off when Agatha turned to finally look at her. Rio’s eyes, usually so bright and glinting, had dulled, searching her gaze repeatedly in time with the soft movements of her fingers against her face. 

Agatha turned away, staring into the distance of her bedroom. She couldn’t look at that face. Not after they had just ignored her son; after they refused to help her get to him. 

As she stared, all that had happened before her nap started to come back to her. Rio had stayed over. Rio had made her breakfast. Rio had held her hair back when her head was halfway down the toilet. Rio hadn’t left. 

She had stayed, the moment she had been given the opportunity to do so. 

But Agatha couldn’t shake the place her mind had just taken her to against her will. 

She couldn’t remember the last time she experienced dreaming. There were faint snippets of a childhood nightmare with monsters and other means of the reality of her life manifesting into a world a small child could actually make sense of, but there wasn't another time in the thirty-years-since that she could remember actually having a dream. 

She didn’t think she was capable of it. 

Then again, it was odd to call it a dream. She supposed it was a nightmare; that her son bore witness to the new life she was creating. A life without him. 

There was something her therapist had told her once, about dreaming. That sometimes, when one experiences so much trauma, their brain learns to shut down entirely during sleep. Their mind protects them from experiencing a reality they aren’t ready to face yet. 

Had Rio broken through every crack inside her, so much so that her own mind was ready to face truths she would, not too long ago, have gladly buried six-feet-under?

Her body was escaping her control and it left her unnerved. She would not let her mind have a will of its own. She would not let herself face that reality. 

For all of the promise and calm she had felt before her nap, her chest felt hunkered down by mooring chains tenfold now. Rio was in her bed, but Nicky wasn’t. They were going to have a baby, but it wouldn’t be Nicky. 

This was not something she could do. 

This was not something she wanted. 

“I think you were dreaming.” Rio’s voice roused her from her spiral. It was not a welcome voice; it was like a stray dog that bit out in fear, painful but not meaning to hurt. “You kept talking…”

She felt her body freeze before she was conscious of doing it. Agatha sat up slowly. Her movements were awkward, dancing around Rio’s limbs in an effort to get away. She needed space. She needed to think. She needed to breathe. 

“Agatha?” 

“I need a minute,” her voice murmured, her throat hoarse. 

“Are you ok?” Rio asked. “Are you going to be sick?”

Agatha shook her head, staring at the wall beside her bed, her chest still heaving. 

“Agatha… What's wrong? You… you’re worrying me,” Rio continued. Agatha couldn’t face to turn around and see the way her brows furrowed or the way her eyes swam with confusion. “Let me help you-”

“I don’t need your help, Rio,” Agatha hissed against her teeth. 

Her words tore through any lingering warmth of their past night shared together. 

It didn’t take long for the covers to be lifted or for her feet to find the cold wood of the floor. She wasn’t quite conscious of her body doing it; she only knew she needed to leave.

She could hear Rio calling after her as she left, but the words were as muffled in her ears as they had been when she was dreaming. 

There was a small part of her that hoped she was still dreaming. That she would wake up and the bottomless pit of uncertainty in her stomach would have only been something she had imagined.

But her chest was still tight, her arms shaking and Rio was still calling her name. 

“Agatha-” 

“Fuck off, Rio.” 

She stood at the kitchen counter, cabinets slamming every few seconds as she prepared to make a coffee. When she looked up to see Rio walking into the living area, the mug she held hit the surface just loud enough for Rio to flinch. 

“I told you, I need a minute!” 

She looked up again, just in time to see Rio’s body recoil into the doorway. It was only a brief movement, before her jaw tightened and chest puffed out slightly. It was Rio’s stare that was most unnerving; its concern had been replaced by a cold fury Agatha was not used to. 

“I don’t need you to look after me,” Agatha snapped. “I am capable of doing that myself.” 

Rio scoffed, her head shaking slightly. “Do you want to go and wake up again, because what the fuck is this?” 

“This isn’t going to work.” 

She couldn’t look at Rio as she spoke, instead focusing on the measurements of her coffee.  

“Excuse me?” 

“You heard me,” she muttered under her breath, borderline snarling. 

Agatha was forced to look up when she realised Rio had stepped further into the living room, her sweatshirt sitting lopsided on her shoulder. A woman so warm and innocent in front of her, worlds apart from the ignorance of the woman her mind had conjured up for her. 

The sight of her only left Agatha’s fingers gripping the counter even harder. 

“You’re not getting rid of me that easily, Agatha.”

“I’m sure the police would answer my call if someone were to break and enter.” 

“Whilst wearing the clothes of said person that owns the home in which they’ve supposedly just broken and entered?” 

Agatha snarled and busied herself with steaming milk. 

“Are you going to eat something?” 

“Are you trying to piss me off?” 

“I know you don't need a babysitter, Agatha, but you need to look after yourself better. Your fridge is empty, you’re wearing yourself thin and you’re clearly not sleeping well. Just let me help you-” 

The metal of the milk jug met the marble counter with an overzealous bang, its contents spilling onto the surface and against Agatha’s forearm. She hissed as the heat seared her skin whilst Rio ran to help her, muttering “Jesus Christ” under her breath. 

“Don’t touch it-” Rio said as she moved to inspect the injuries. 

Agatha swiped her hand away, clutched to her chest as she stepped back from Rio. 

“Leave me,” her voice was darker than Rio had ever heard it before, “alone.”

Rio’s hand froze as her eyes fell to the floor. She backed away slowly like a trainer would to a rabid dog. Agatha couldn’t stifle her humourless laugh; there was something about becoming the bad guy that people believed she already was. It was an odd comfort. 

“Agatha…” 

The pain of Agatha's arm was a sweet distraction from the turmoil within her head. 

“So this is how it's going to be?” 

“What?” Agatha spat. 

“I just have to sit back and watch you hurt yourself?” 

“I don’t just throw hot milk on myself for fun, Rio.” 

Rio closed her eyes and willed a deep breath. She had stepped far away from Agatha; the kitchen island now a no-man's-land between them. 

“That’s not what I mean and you know it,” she replied. “Is it going to take another miscarriage scare for you to start listening to me? Because that’s where you’re headed if you keep this up.” 

Agatha’s eyes snapped up at Rio, glaring and deep. 

“You’re not looking after yourself, Agatha,” Rio said. “And it’s not just you that you need to look after now. You’re responsible for something that would kill me if we lost, but the only thing I can do to prevent that is to look after you. Let me look after you. Please.” 

She wished, deep down, more than anything, that she could give Rio the acceptance she so desperately wanted. It was what she deserved, but how could Agatha trust her? She couldn’t even trust her own mind, let alone another person. She didn’t know how to fall into waiting arms, she only knew how to bare her teeth and show her hackles. 

“Don’t play me that card, Rio,” Agatha’s voice was resigned, her movements slow as she ran cold water on her burning skin. At least it was one way of showing she could look after herself. “You have known about it for all of five minutes. You didn’t have to experience the bleeding, you didn’t have to experience that not-knowing. Don’t you even dare.” 

“Then why are you acting like it’s fine if it happens again?” 

She should have bitten her tongue; she should have walked out of her own apartment and never returned. She wished that the last twenty-four hours had also been a dream, that she would wake up soon and her secret was still safe with her. That none of this was her reality. 

“If it happens again, it’ll be because of you. Because of the stress you are causing me.” 

The hurt on Rio's face should have hurt her too.

“Don’t you dare put the blame on me-”

“It doesn’t feel that nice, does it?” 

Rio laughed with a bitterness that had every inch of Agatha's skin feeling cold. “I don’t think you have known, for one second in your whole damn life, what it is like to blame yourself for losing something, Agatha. For someone.” 

The words cut a little too close to home for Agatha. Her jaw clenched, fists balled together. Gone were the snappy retorts and bitter remarks. What took their place was much, much worse. 

“Are you sure about that, Rio?” 

Agatha’s voice was low, a menacing tone that Rio didn’t know she was capable of. She had used that voice before, but never at Rio, and it left the hairs on the back of Rio’s neck raised.

“I have watched my parents never come home. I have witnessed my abuelita take her last breath. I have had to hear the screams of my tía when her two boys didn’t make it. And mi prima’s infant son, too. I have seen what it is like to lose a child, Agatha. You wouldn’t survive it.” 

She barely registered what Rio had said. There was a recurring voice calling out in her head; a little boy screaming for his mother. There were intermittent, clinical and cold beeps that hurt her brain. A doctor kept telling her, “I’m sorry, there is nothing more we can do apart from make sure he is comfortable.” 

“Leave me alone,” Agatha muttered under her breath.  

“No, you don’t get to keep doing this.” 

The beeping in her head got louder. Nicky’s screams rattled in her chest. 

Agatha’s voice hardened. “Leave.” 

“I’m not losing you, and I’m not losing our baby.” 

I’m sorry. There is nothing more we can do apart from make sure he is comfortable. 

Agatha laughed bitterly. “You’ll cope just fine.” 

“Do you even understand loss, Agatha? Or have you spent so much time in that little self-preserving bubble of yours that you really have lost your heart?”

Agatha launched her mug at the wall. 

It shattered, just like the heart Rio believed she had lost. 

Rio flinched, her own fists clenched like she was preparing for the worst. 

Mama, it hurts. 

Mama, I don’t like it. 

Mama, don’t leave me. 

Agatha couldn't get the voices out of her head. Voices she had long ago forbade herself to think about, now looping around in her mind like they wanted to taunt her; like they wanted her to break. 

And break she did. 

“I have lost everything!” 

Agatha’s words weren’t words anymore. Only a harrowing scream from five years of keeping it all within her. 

That was when the tears came. They were ugly, breathless and heaving. 

“I couldn’t save him!”

A nearby plate was launched onto the floor next. 

Then her knees buckled beneath her. 

Rio crossed the room in a few steps, any fear or hesitate within her replaced by the sheer need to act and to help. Rio was muttering something as Agatha felt her arms envelope around her body. She tried to wrestle against the hold she was in. Tried to hit something; hit anything to make the pain inside her ease.

“I couldn’t save him!” 

Her cry was broken, just like she was upon the kitchen floor. 

“Hey, look at me,” Rio reached for Agatha’s arm that had escaped her, she moved her leg to lock onto one of Agatha’s own in attempt to calm her erratic movements. 

“Leave me alone,” Agatha choked out as she squirmed against Rio’s chest. “Let me go!” 

“You’re ok,” Rio said softly once she had secured her hold. She kept repeating the phrase into Agatha’s hair, soft and calm as she wrapped both arms around her neck, pulling the distraught woman into her chest. “I’m here, Agatha. Focus on your breathing.” 

“I can’t… I can’t-” 

Agatha could barely form the words, let alone catch her own breath. 

“Rio, I lost him-” she sniffed, her head buried into Rio's chest as each one of her limbs gave in to their previous tension. “I lost him, I couldn’t save him… I lost …I-”

Rio shushed her gently, gnawing at her lip as she tried to calm the crying woman in her arms. 

“I didn’t know-” Agatha hiccuped, “I didn’t know… how to… how to tell you.” 

“Just focus on breathing, ok?” Rio continued to tell her with a kindness Agatha knew she did not deserve. “You’re ok. I’m not leaving. Just breathe.” 

“I don’t want to do this again. I can’t be a good mom.” 

“Hey, that’s not true, but you don’t need to think about that now. Just focus on breathing. In for four, hold for four, out for six. With me, you ready?” 

But Agatha was inconsolable. Rio didn’t know that Agatha Harkness was capable of feeling this strongly, let alone breaking so harshly. 

Still, Rio didn’t let go of her. 

One hand combed through Agatha’s hair, keeping it out from her tear-streaked face. Another hand rubbed against her back, slow and soothing. 

“We’re just going to calm down, ok? Just let me hold you. Just sit here. You don’t need to talk. You don’t need to explain yourself. Just know, I am not leaving you, Agatha.” 

Agatha could only cry more. She had verbally berated this woman; had lied to her; had mistreated her in a way no one deserved, let alone someone as good as Rio, and she still refused to leave her side. 

Rio was helping her too, not that she would admit it openly. The beeping of the monitor had faded and her son’s voice had returned to her heart instead of her head. She could feel warmth, and oddly, a relief from her sudden outburst. 

She may not have said it explicitly, but everything was now out in the open. 

 

 

Rio gave her time to calm her heaving breaths into slower, shallower ones. She didn’t rush to pick Agatha up from the floor, but when she did, she carefully guided her around the broken shards of china and onto the couch. She told Agatha to stay put, to drink some water, that she would be back in a few minutes.

Agatha watched Rio out of the corner of her eye as she swept away the broken mug and shattered plate. When Rio disappeared into her bedroom, she heard voices but was too numb to care. 

“What do I do?” Agatha heard Rio speak, her footsteps pacing back and forth enough to reach her ears. “Has this happened before? What does she need?” 

It could have been a few minutes or a few hours by the time Rio returned. Agatha couldn’t keep track, all she could focus on was ensuring she took one breath after another, no matter how little she wanted to. 

She couldn’t look up until a soft, scratchy weight was placed over her lap. It was green and white and golden and only succeeded in producing more tears from her. 

“Jen said you’d want this blanket,” Rio said softly. “She said it's usually under your pillow but I couldn’t find it, until I remembered seeing it earlier this morning in your closet.” 

Agatha brought the crocheted material to her face, breathing it in, her fingers twisting into the fraying patches. It didn’t take long for the blanket to soak up her tears and ease her breath. 

“It’s beautiful,” Rio remarked gently. With caution, she moved to perch on the coffee table, close enough in case Agatha needed her, whilst maintaining a distance she knew the woman needed even more. “Will you tell me who it belongs to?” 

Agatha tried to speak but nothing came out apart from a choked sob. Her face found the blanket once more, her eyes squeezed shut as she remembered the little life who used to keep it warm. 

A hand eased onto her knee, causing Agatha to look up and get lost in deep, dark eyes gazing up at her. There was no anger, no fear, no hatred behind them. 

She was, once again, reminded that Rio Vidal had not left her. 

The silence stretched thin, their eyes searching each other. Agatha’s hand found her chest as she took a deep, pained breath. 

“...My son,” she whispered. 

The grip on her knee tightened into a reassuring squeeze. Rio’s dimples were on show, her smile so soft. She gave a soft nod, a permission to Agatha that this space was safe; that she was safe. 

“His name was Nicky.” 

She could see Rio biting her cheek just like she could hear her breath stilling, but her eyes never lost their softness nor their openness. She was still safe. She was still safe. 

“...Was?” 

Agatha’s cheek glistened from a single tear escaping her eye. 

“I lost him in 2019,” she took another breath, her fingers curling into the blanket tightly. “He was nearly four years old.” 

“Agatha…” Rio’s own eyes were glassy, “I’m so, so sorry.” 

“That’s why you never heard back from me for all those years. That’s why I… I ignored you. I didn’t think you’d be… be interested in it all. And then… then he-” she choked, trying to disguise it as a cough. “I wasn’t the same afterwards. I don’t think I ever will be.” 

Rio took Agatha’s hand into her own, cupping it gently. She slowly bent down, her eyes locked into Agatha’s as she placed the softest kiss to her knuckles. 

Rio’s lips curled into a small smile against Agatha’s hand. “Will you tell me about him?” 

Agatha couldn’t breathe. She couldn’t think. She could only stare into those hazel eyes and feel their warmth soak into her cold being. She eventually offered a nod, her hands clutching Nicky’s blanket against her chin. 

“When you’re ready,” Rio continued, returning Agatha’s hand back to her knee, under the soft material. “First, I just want you to breathe. Jen is coming over soon with groceries, and before you argue, she wouldn’t take no for an answer. She promised she would leave us alone after.” 

It took a few minutes for Agatha to collect herself and gather her breath into a semi-functional pattern before she could speak again. The racing thoughts turned to a calmer, dull throb at the back of her head. Her breath slowed to an occasional hiccup and deep inhale. Rio’s hand never left her knee; it was like she was offering all the calm and goodness in herself for Agatha to siphon.

“You called Jen?” 

It was only upon the second time of hearing her best friend’s name that Agatha realised it must have been Jen on the phone when Rio had disappeared. 

She watched Rio’s face fall at her words, briefly, before biting at her lip. 

It hit Agatha again that Rio had chosen to stay, even after seeing her in such a state.  

“I don’t have Lilia’s number and… I didn’t know what else to do. I’m sorry, Agatha, I was really worried and-” 

“Rio,” Agatha cut her off softly. “Thank you.” 

“She didn’t tell me… about Nicky. She said that it wasn’t her place, but she knew what to do. Was she there, with you, through it all?” 

Agatha nodded, taking another deep breath. “Her and Lilia. His entire life.” 

“I’m glad you had them.” 

“Me too.” 

“You have me too, now. Ok?”

Agatha’s words were left dry in her mouth. Her heart hurt, her head pounding. She was sure her eyes would fail her again. “I don’t deserve that, Rio.” 

“There are many things you don’t deserve, but none of those things include me.” 

Their eyes met in a deep stare for several beats, until Agatha shocked even herself by breaking into a small laugh. “Thanks, Shakespeare.” 

“I’m trying to be nice!” 

“I prefer it when you're angry at me.” 

“I’m sure that won’t take long,” Rio muttered under her breath before smirking. “Get some rest, I’m going to shower if that’s ok? And steal more of your clothes. Jen will be over soon.” 

Agatha nodded softly. "Ok. There's fresh towels in the cupboard." 

"Thank you," Rio said, before pausing, and staring into her with a deepness that told her their joking was over. "And thank you, for telling me. We'll talk more when you're ready, ok?"

She placed a kiss on Agatha's hair before leaving the room, leaving Agatha’s chest feeling unnervingly full despite the lead-like exhaustion of her bones and body. 

She lay down, her body curled into itself and Nicky’s blanket enveloping her tightly. Her hand found her stomach, consciously for perhaps the first ever time. 

She had both her babies with her, and a woman prepared to stand by her and love both of them. She had to be better, for all of them.

 

 

It took twenty minutes of staring at the ceiling before Rio returned. Agatha had tried to close her eyes and get some sleep but every time she did, she would only see his face and hear his screams for her. 

Rio had stolen a new sweater, a plain and navy one with a pair of gray sweatpants that Agatha didn’t even remember owning. She wouldn’t be caught dead in something like that, but at least they now had a new owner. 

Another kiss, so gentle she hadn’t even realised Rio had walked over to her, was placed upon her head. “Your shower is incredible. It’s bigger than my entire bathroom.” 

“I know,” Agatha murmured, a small smile on her lips. “I suppose it could fit two people quite comfortably.” 

Agatha sat herself up despite the exhaustion working against her and caught Rio looking at her with curled lips and raised brows as she sat down at the end of the couch Agatha already occupied. “You haven’t tried it out?” 

“You were the last person I showered with, back in that dingy crack-den apartment of yours.” 

“Oh god, and the faucet-”

“Would randomly rattle throughout the night? Don’t remind me.” 

“You’ve not showered with anyone in a decade?” 

“Pregnancy isn’t known to leave a woman’s body looking overly alluring.” 

“Oh,” Rio mumbled, as if she was embarrassed she had never considered that before. That she had never considered that Agatha Harkness was capable of feeling insecure. “That’s why you’ve always been covered up since… that’s why you… why you left me that night.” 

Agatha nodded, trying to ignore the guilt she felt at Rio bringing up the night she had abruptly left Rio's apartment only a few weeks ago.

“I look different, Rio.” 

“I don’t care how you look,” Rio said quickly, moving closer to Agatha. 

Before she could continue, a knock at the front door interrupted them. 

Rio sighed, her hand finding Agatha’s knee. “I am not finished,” she said, leaning closer to Agatha as her eyes bore deep into blue. “I am going to make sure you know how goddamn attractive you are. I promise.” 

Agatha tried to hide the reddening of her cheeks in the blanket, her chest doing somersaults against her will. She watched Rio walk away, offering a roll of her eyes when Rio winked at her over her shoulder. 

“Hey,” she heard Jen say from down the hallway. “How’s she doing?” 

There was rustling of paper bags and some muttered words she couldn’t entirely hear. 

“I’ve never… I've never seen her like that Jen,” Rio’s voice was unfortunately betrayed by the echoes of Agatha’s home. Her stomach clenched slightly at the words. “I had no idea about any of it. She’s carrying so much.”

Jen responded with something unintelligible, and Agatha struggled to stop her eyes from rolling again. Perhaps Rio was right to be so concerned, because Agatha didn’t have the energy or will to get up from that couch and remind them who she was.

It read, Ask Me His Name. 

It was about a mother’s own experience losing her son. 

“I bought a couple,” Rio’s voice had become smaller, her hands hidden in her sleeves. “They’re by the fruit bowl.”

Ever since she had experienced the scare several weeks ago when she found she was bleeding, Agatha didn’t trust her body to keep her baby safe. It only made it worse that she had Rio’s expectations to up hold now also. 

Her biggest fear was being sat on that cold, hard examination table in Jen’s office, watching as Rio’s eyes changed from wonder to pure, unadulterated obliteration. “I was just taking them,” Agatha said with a flat smile.

She wasn’t just taking them; she had completely forgotten about them. 

Vitamins were the last thing on her mind with Rio looking like that. It was perhaps the first thing that morning that had stopped her from being a pit of doom and gloom. 

She tried to hide the somersaults within her at the sight of Rio. Was it the dungarees, or was it the hormones? She’d never know.

Agatha Harkness: Rio is coming today. Don’t say a fucking word. 

Jennifer Kale: You can’t do that to me!

“Ok, ok. Sorry.” Rio sighed, her hand rubbing up and down Agatha’s arm. “Querida, whatever happens, I’ll be there with you.” 

Rio had never called her that before. 

She didn't know how she felt about it. 

Actually, she did know how she felt about it. She just hadn’t worked out if she liked the gooey, warm feeling it sparked inside of her or not. 

The receptionist at the clinic had learned to let her just walk through, offering nothing but a scowl and beady eyes. Rio had offered a warm greeting, only to be stared down as well, and quickly proceed, upon catching up to Agatha, to murmur, ‘what the hell was her problem?” 

“Oh…” Agatha shrugged, her palms outstretched in question. “She always looks like a bitch. Got a right stick up her ass, don’t worry about it.”

What the hell did they have to go through? 

This was a routine scan. Throw the gel on, get the doppler out and fucking voila: baby. 

She didn’t need a chit-chat, she just needed to hear the goddamn heartbeat of this thing inside her that had been keeping her up at all hours of the night, worried sick that they didn’t even exist anymore. 

Rio, naturally, had sprung at the opportunity at a sit-down discussion over their future offspring. She hadn’t stopped going on about this appointment. Agatha had found it endearing, she couldn’t deny but it didn’t leave her feeling any less nauseous. 

“Hi,” Jen breathed out a smile as she sat down across the desk from them. 

It felt weird. 

That was her best friend opposite her, and Jen had her sitting there like she was in some couples counselling session that she absolutely had not signed up for. 

“How have you both been?”

“Well fucking propose then.” 

Rio coughed, then proceeded to choke on her cough and become a spluttering mess.

Was she kidding? 

She had to be kidding. 

Hormones, Agatha, was all she could keep telling herself.

It wasn’t so much an aversion… more that she had once met the spawn of satan as a child and said spawn of satan came in the body of a few millimetres’ long metal stick.

And Rio?

Agatha wished she could have bottled up what she witnessed happen to Rio’s face. If she thought her reaction to the pregnancy was magical, she had no words for whatever this was.

July 8th.

A date that had never meant anything to her before. A day that had never meant anything to her, or to Rio, and suddenly it was about to become the most important day of their lives. 

Agatha just had to get herself and their baby to that finish line now.

The cheek of her.

That was a conversation for another evening, preferably one void of two women who would practically be foaming at the mouth if they ever caught wind of Agatha and Rio sharing said conversation. 

Perhaps she hadn’t given enough thought into giving someone a key before establishing if they were, or were not, actually a couple first.

Agatha’s jaw parted. “Honey?” 

“She popped the question, a couple of days ago now. It’s official,” Jen smiled. 

Rio and Agatha’s eyes met, both sharing an internal question that sounding something along the lines of, “what the fuck are we witnessing right now?” 

“Woah, hold up,” Rio began to laugh. “You’re getting married?” 

“No!” Jen and Alice exclaimed at the same time. 

“Thank god,” Agatha muttered. “You won’t see me getting into a nice dress for at least the next two years.” 

“Anyway, you two are having a baby. How can you judge us?”

“What Jen is trying to say is,” Alice elbowed her discreetly. “We’re dating. Officially.” 

They offered their congratulations and the moment their hosts began to clear the table, Rio leaned over into Agatha’s ear. 

“We are so talking about this when we get home.” 

Home. 

She could barely laugh; Rio’s slip of the tongue hit her square in the chest. 

During dessert, the inevitable questions were asked. Agatha quickly realised she had been foolish to hope that they could skirt over the elephant in the room - or child in utero, for that matter. 

“Oh, I have the scan,” Rio’s face lit up as she reached into her pocket, retrieved her wallet and unravelled the grainy, black and white image. “That’s them,” she showed it to Alice, pointing out the different features of their baby with Jen’s help. 

Agatha let Rio have that moment for herself. 

“What am I looking at?” Alice’s eyes squinted. “It looks like a peanut…” she turned to Jen. “This is what you do all day? Play guess the anatomy of a blob?” 

“For gods sake, no, Alice.”

“They’re the size of a plum,” Rio said proudly. “Our lil’ plum.” 

Agatha sighed, smiling nonetheless. “Rio-” 

Alice leaned back in her chair, watching them both. Agatha could feel her eyes on her. 

“You both seem happy,” she said softly. 

Rio found Agatha’s eyes, and Agatha’s eyes returned the permission to her that she was looking for. “We are,” Rio nodded. 

She only hoped that it could continue. 

“Agatha,” Alice spoke up eventually. “I… I wanted to talk to you about what happened on New Year's Eve.” 

Agatha’s smile was flat. “Oh, good-ey!” 

“I owe you an apology,” she said, and Agatha had to hold back her scoff through fear that Rio’s shoe would find her shin with a terrifying precision. “When Ralph cornered you… Well, I honestly had my hackles up the whole night. So when I saw you with him, I thought you were just screwing Rio over.” 

Agatha’s fingers flicked against her chin, listening on with intrigue. 

“I didn’t know you were pregnant.” 

“No one did,” Agatha reminded her. “And they weren’t meant to, for that matter.” 

Alice’s cheeks turned a shade of red that Agatha hadn’t seen before. “And I didn’t know that he forced that drink into your hand.”

She thought back to that moment - a moment she had genuinely feared for the life within her. She thought back to his sickly scent of sweat and the way his eyes darkened as if there was nothing behind them. 

“You didn’t exactly give me a chance,” Agatha said. 

Alice winced. “No, I didn’t… What I am trying to say is, I am sorry. I should never have done that. And I want to be there for both of you… for this, if you’ll let me,” she indicated with her hands; Agatha tried to pretend it wasn’t directly at her stomach. “... this journey you’re both about to go on. I want to be someone you can count on.” 

Rio and Jen shifted in their seats, watching the uncomfortable exchange, ready to swoop in if the moment demanded it. 

Agatha sat still, watching Alice for a while. 

Just enough to put her on edge again. It was the least she deserved. 

“Fine.” 

“Fine?” Alice blinked. “That’s it?” 

“What do you want, a certificate or something?” 

Both of their lips curled as they stared each other down, until Alice started to laugh, then Jen, and then Rio. 

Agatha exhaled as Jen moved the conversation swiftly on. 

As their night drew to a close, Rio’s hand found Agatha’s beneath the table. Her fingers gently grazed over Agatha’s knuckles whilst neither of them drew attention to it. Rio just threaded their palms together and carried on talking about something entirely unrelated. 

It didn’t leave the hairs on the back of her neck standing up. In fact, it allowed her to ease back into her chair more, as she squeezed back once. 

It felt like they had been doing it forever. 

And Agatha thought, maybe, she could keep doing it forever.



Chapter End Notes

i have not read this over but hey, the world is falling apart so there’s bigger fish to fry
